# V1 support record for Agentic Lean Campaigns

**Audit date:** 2026-08-26  
**Status:** read-only v1 record; clean-v2 design input  
**Normative machine companion:** `V1_SUPPORT.json`  
**Primary decision:** preserve v1 and its evidence corpus read-only; implement a minimal native v2 beside it; migrate only after behavioral, evidence, mathematical, and assurance gates pass.

> This record is intentionally dense. It is not a tutorial or a claim that CMV Conjecture 3.12 is solved. It distinguishes controller completion, mathematical closure, formal scope, evidence integrity, and assurance throughout.


## 1. Executive decision

**Decision:** implement native v2 as a **clean parallel implementation beside v1**, using a v1 corpus that must first be **frozen, content-addressed, and pinned** as a read-only executable specification and migration oracle. This is **not** a destructive rewrite of historical v1 bundles, **not** an in-place refactor of the v1 control plane, and **not** a program of further v1 polishing. Existing symbol-shaped readers belong only in a quarantined compatibility boundary such as `v1_import` / `v2.compat_v1`; they must preserve observed v1 acceptance and rejection behavior, read immutable bytes, emit migration diagnostics, and write **only distinct v2 records**. No native v2 path may emit a new v1 record, silently reinterpret a v1 snapshot, or mutate a retained v1 run.

The rebuild decision follows from structural boundaries visible in the supplied corpus:

- `src/campaign_builder/runtime.py::CampaignBuilder.run`, `CampaignBuilder.resume`, `_execute`, `_consume`, `_reset_stages`, `_route_retry`, `approve_handoff`, `verify_campaign_bundle`, `replay_verifier_command`, and `refresh_campaign_evidence` combine scheduling, durable state, workspace transactions, evidence, approval, replay, process/Herdr recovery, and status projection in one controller.
- `src/campaign_builder/features.py::FeatureRegistry`, `FeatureContext`, `FeatureResult`, and the feature IDs `agent`, `command`, `lean_contract`, `claim_ledger`, `semantic_contract`, and `artifact_promote` combine plugin registration, execution adaptation, artifact conventions, retry routing, and mathematical closure policy behind an unversioned in-memory boundary.
- `src/campaign_builder/agent_runner.py::execute` and `src/campaign_builder/command.py::run_captured_command` independently implement launch, stream pumping, timeout/quota polling, process termination, registry interaction, secret cleanup, and receipt production; `agent_runner.py` already imports the private `command.py::_process_exited_without_reaping` and `command.py::_signal_process_group` interfaces while retaining different semantics.
- `src/campaign_builder/journal.py::append_state_snapshot` and `load_state_snapshot` treat `events.jsonl` as a hash-linked authority, while `src/campaign_builder/inspection.py::inspect_assurance`, `src/campaign_builder/cli.py`, and runtime status paths consume `state.json` directly. `src/campaign_builder/artifacts.py::verify_evidence_index` proves retained-file self-consistency but does not establish journal/projection equivalence.
- `src/campaign_builder/config.py::CampaignSpec.load` and `CampaignSpec.load_resolved` read incompatible source and resolved shapes both labeled `schema_version=1`; the resolved reader restores current defaults and performs weaker checks than the source reader. `src/campaign_builder/project.py::ProjectSpec.load` imports `config.py::ExecutionSpec`, so project-v1 behavior inherits campaign execution defaults and parser defects without an independent schema identity.
- `src/campaign_builder/models.py::CampaignOutcome.parse` and `CampaignOutcome.validate_against`, `src/campaign_builder/claims.py::build_claim_ledger`, and `src/campaign_builder/inspection.py::inspect_assurance` do not implement one sound final closure predicate: a model-supplied `solved` status can coexist with rejected obligations or weak evidence, failed claim-ledger producers can contribute closure, and unrelated Lean/semantic stage counts can contribute assurance.
- `src/campaign_builder/process_registry.py` and `src/campaign_builder/processes.py` retain incompatible schema-v1 controller/process/unit records and duplicate `/proc`, liveness, and process-group behavior; `src/campaign_builder/processes.py::stop_all_campaigns` is not one atomic or resumable transition machine.
- `src/campaign_builder/runtime.py::_restore_checkpoint`, `src/campaign_builder/publication.py::_recover_pending_publication`, `src/campaign_builder/handoff.py::digest_handoff`, and live-workspace replay in `src/campaign_builder/runtime.py::replay_verifier_command` expose foundational crash, parse/hash, and mutation-boundary defects that cannot be converted into a trustworthy v2 substrate by local cleanup alone.

V2 must retain the useful v1 concepts but assign them to distinct native boundaries: contracts and schema registry; immutable store and authoritative events; pure state machine and serial scheduler; one execution supervisor and resource ledger; assurance derivation; and publication transaction. The initial native v2 core should correspond to the supplied `v2.contracts`, `v2.store`, `v2.control`, `v2.execution`, `v2.assurance`, `v2.publication`, and `v2.facade` boundaries, with `v2.compat_v1` as the only package permitted to import v1 schema implementations.

The decision also fixes vocabulary. **Execution**, **evidence**, **mathematics**, and **assurance** are independent dimensions:

- A successful process result from `src/campaign_builder/agent_runner.py::main`, `src/campaign_builder/command.py::run_captured_command`, or a feature executor establishes only an execution outcome.
- A hash-valid bundle under `src/campaign_builder/artifacts.py::verify_evidence_index` establishes retained-set consistency, not writer authenticity, mathematical truth, or release assurance.
- A complete configured stage set under `src/campaign_builder/runtime.py::CampaignBuilder` does not establish `MATHEMATICALLY_SOLVED`; the operations record explicitly includes campaign-complete runs whose `src/campaign_builder/models.py::CampaignOutcome` remained `unsolved`.
- A focused pass such as `python -m pytest -q tests/test_semantic.py` or `python -m py_compile ...` establishes only `FOCUSED_TEST_PASSED`, not `CODE_COMPLETE`, `FULL_GATES_PASSED`, or `ASSURANCE_PASSED`.

## 2. Scope, method, and evidence classes

### Scope

This record covers the supplied v1 architecture under `src/campaign_builder/`:

- **Contract ingestion and model records:** `config.py`, `project.py`, `models.py`, and `handoff.py`.
- **Retained evidence and state:** `artifacts.py`, `journal.py`, `claims.py`, `semantic.py`, `inspection.py`, and `metrics.py`.
- **Execution and resource control:** `agent_runner.py`, `command.py`, `process_registry.py`, `processes.py`, `sandbox.py`, and `herdr.py`.
- **Campaign control and feature dispatch:** `runtime.py` and `features.py`.
- **Formal verification and publication:** `lean.py` and `publication.py`.
- **Outer orchestration and interfaces:** `regime.py`, `autonomy.py`, `benchmark.py`, `cli.py`, `terminal_status.py`, `__init__.py`, and `__main__.py`.
- **Supporting policy surfaces:** `final_reporter.md`, `inter_campaign_optimizer.md`, `planner.md`, `research_gate.md`, `researcher.md`, benchmark fixtures, examples, root documentation, `regression.py`, `math_tools/exact.py`, `numeric.py`, `symbolic.py`, and `references.py`.

The architecture/control summaries supplied no numeric source locations, so those sections are grounded by exact path, symbol, schema identifier, and described behavior. The incident history retains a limited set of historical line ranges from reviewed moving-tree revisions; they are not frozen-current locators and must be re-established on the pinned commit. No complete frozen repository, full test suite result, package/consumer inventory, controller state schema, publisher integration run, supported platform matrix, or comprehensive threat model was supplied. Claims about release readiness, performance at scale, hidden consumers, and deployment enforcement must remain unresolved rather than inferred.

### Method

The method is contract reconstruction rather than intent reconstruction:

1. Treat public readers, writers, DTOs, schema IDs, retained paths, status strings, and imported private helpers as observable v1 behavior. Examples include `src/campaign_builder/config.py::CampaignSpec.load`, `src/campaign_builder/handoff.py::handoff_prompt_contract`, `src/campaign_builder/artifacts.py::verify_evidence_index`, `src/campaign_builder/journal.py::load_state_snapshot`, `src/campaign_builder/agent_runner.py::main`, and `src/campaign_builder/runtime.py::campaign_status_exit_code`.
2. Preserve exact v1 compatibility facts in read-only adapters, including defects such as `src/campaign_builder/config.py::CampaignSpec.load_resolved` restoring defaults, `src/campaign_builder/semantic.py::SemanticReview.to_dict` omitting `schema_version`, `src/campaign_builder/features.py::FeatureRegistry(features=())` selecting defaults through falsey coercion, and unversioned `src/campaign_builder/handoff.py::ReviewDecision` and agent-runner requests.
3. Separate reusable invariants from implementations that violate or overextend them. For example, retain the hash-chain invariant of `src/campaign_builder/journal.py` but not full-chain replay on every append; retain same-directory atomic replacement from `src/campaign_builder/artifacts.py::atomic_write_text` but add explicit ownership/mode semantics; retain digest-bound handoff approval from `src/campaign_builder/runtime.py::approve_handoff` but not its incomplete bundle validation.
4. Treat described tests and runs as bounded observations only. They do not upgrade a source reading into universal assurance.
5. Require v2 semantic tightening to be explicit in migration reports. Compatibility parity means reproducing v1 reader behavior in `v2.compat_v1`, not importing v1 weaknesses into native v2 contracts.

### Evidence classes and non-substitution rules

| Class | What the supplied record can establish | What it cannot establish |
|---|---|---|
| **Source-grounded architecture evidence** | Exact visible behavior and coupling at paths such as `src/campaign_builder/runtime.py::CampaignBuilder`, `src/campaign_builder/features.py::FeatureRegistry`, `src/campaign_builder/lean.py::run_proof_gate`, and `src/campaign_builder/publication.py::publish_campaign`. | Runtime behavior on an unspecified frozen commit, hidden-consumer compatibility, or successful failure recovery. |
| **Focused execution evidence** | The retained `tests/test_semantic.py` execution and `py_compile` execution passed for `SemanticBindingFix`. | Full suite, integration, formatting, linting, type checking, Lake/Lean qualification, fault injection, or release assurance. |
| **Campaign execution evidence** | Run `20260815T202603Z-dc18b6` completed its configured nine required stages and approved handoff; run `20260824T091317Z-1cbb51` completed required stages while recording `unsolved`; run `20260824T205221Z-b63aa6` stopped downstream work after a continuation-gate `stop`. | Mathematical closure, external publication completion, full boundary-measure equality, theorem reconstruction, or implementation assurance. |
| **Retained artifact/evidence evidence** | `src/campaign_builder/artifacts.py::digest_file`, `digest_tree`, and `verify_evidence_index` can establish file hash/size and exact-set properties under their v1 rules; `src/campaign_builder/journal.py` can establish canonical hash-link consistency under its v1 rules. | Authenticity against a writer able to rewrite the bundle, anti-replay across campaign/run identities unless explicitly bound, semantic correctness, or release approval. |
| **Formal/mathematical evidence** | `src/campaign_builder/lean.py::ProofGateResult`, `AxiomReport`, `run_proof_gate`, claim verdicts from `src/campaign_builder/claims.py`, and reviews from `src/campaign_builder/semantic.py` represent distinct formal and semantic evidence lanes. | The exact CMV theorem or source-faithful full-domain claim; the supplied operations record leaves the series `MATHEMATICAL_UNSOLVED` and identifies open Lean trust blockers. |
| **Assurance/review evidence** | Scoped adversarial reviews identified publication, recovery, secret, process-identity, Lean-trust, campaign-data, and coherence defects; current-tree rereads withdrew stale findings where exact symbols had changed. | `ASSURANCE_PASSED`: no single frozen commit has complete retained full gates, fault matrices, and independent post-fix reviews with no open P0/P1. |

The permanent status discipline is therefore:

- `PROCESS_SUCCEEDED` concerns a subprocess or adapter receipt from paths such as `src/campaign_builder/command.py` or `src/campaign_builder/agent_runner.py`.
- `CAMPAIGN_TERMINAL` concerns controller status from `src/campaign_builder/runtime.py`, `src/campaign_builder/regime.py`, or `src/campaign_builder/autonomy.py`; it is not necessarily `CAMPAIGN_COMPLETE`.
- `CAMPAIGN_COMPLETE` concerns an adopted plan, acceptable required-stage semantics, required evidence/handoffs, and reconciled children; it does not imply `MATHEMATICALLY_SOLVED`.
- `MATHEMATICALLY_SOLVED` requires the exact frozen target, complete obligations and coverage, exact Lean type/axiom evidence, source/bridge closure, paired semantic evidence, and valid retained provenance.
- `ASSURANCE_PASSED` concerns required build/test/security/crash/coherence gates on one frozen candidate; it cannot be inferred from source inspection, campaign completion, mathematical status, or a reviewer process exiting zero.

## 3. V1 architecture and lifecycle

### Contract and schema plane

`src/campaign_builder/config.py::CampaignSpec.load` is the campaign source authority for TOML `schema_version=1`; it constructs `CampaignInput`, `StageSpec`, `HandoffSpec`, `PublishSpec`, `ExecutionSpec`, `TargetSpec`, `ObligationSpec`, and `CampaignSpec`, resolves filesystem/environment-dependent values, checks IDs and targets, validates the stage DAG and research/build boundary through `_validate_handoff` and `_validate_stages`, checks obligation evidence-stage references, and invokes `_reject_inline_secret_values`. `CampaignSpec.stage_order` recomputes a deterministic lexicographic topological order.

`src/campaign_builder/config.py::CampaignSpec.to_resolved_dict` emits a different resolved JSON shape, also called `schema_version=1`, and injects instruction paths into `stage.config`; `CampaignSpec.load_resolved` reads that shape with weaker validation and default restoration. `src/campaign_builder/project.py::ProjectSpec.load` parses project TOML v1, delegates runtime policy to `config.py::ExecutionSpec.from_table`, and supports effective overrides through `ProjectSpec.with_compute_profile` without full coupled-limit revalidation or distinct effective-profile provenance.

Model artifacts are independently versioned in `src/campaign_builder/models.py`: research decision/result v1, orchestration plan v3 through `OrchestrationPlan.parse`, continuation decision v1 through `ContinuationDecision.parse_text`, and campaign outcome v2 through `CampaignOutcome.parse`. `OrchestrationPlan.parse` validates task and obligation references, the task DAG, phases, continuation-gate ancestry, and caller bounds; `CampaignOutcome.validate_against` checks obligation IDs and promotion ancestry but does not derive a complete final success predicate.

`src/campaign_builder/handoff.py::handoff_prompt_contract` and `digest_handoff` recognize exactly `research-v1`, `claim-proposals-v1`, `claim-verdicts-v1`, and `semantic-review-v1`. Validation is distributed across `ResearchHandoff.load`, `src/campaign_builder/claims.py::ClaimProposalBundle.load`, `ClaimVerdictBundle.load`, and `src/campaign_builder/semantic.py::SemanticReview.load`; `src/campaign_builder/config.py::HandoffSpec.schema` accepts generic identifiers, so manifest validation and runtime registration are not one closed registry.

### Run-store and control plane

`src/campaign_builder/runtime.py::CampaignBuilder.run` creates the retained tree containing `configuration/`, `input-snapshot/`, `workspace/`, `agents/`, `stages/`, `logs/`, `prompts/`, `recovery/`, `state.json`, `events.jsonl`, `evidence.json`, `input-manifest.json`, `.campaign.lock`, optional `replays/`, and promoted outputs. `campaign_run_lock` uses `O_NOFOLLOW|O_CLOEXEC`, checks a regular lock file, and takes a nonblocking exclusive `flock`; this coordinates cooperating local POSIX processes but does not confine parent traversal or hostile same-owner writers.

Initialization retains resolved configuration and instructions, snapshots or records inputs, writes manifests, initializes journal-backed state and evidence, provisions controller/Herdr state, and enters `CampaignBuilder._execute`. The setup sequence is not one transaction, so interruption can leave a partially initialized run tree.

Scheduling is a DAG in configuration but serial in execution. `CampaignBuilder._ready_stages` finds pending stages whose dependencies succeeded and mutates descendants to `skipped` when prerequisites fail; `_execute` selects `ready[0]`. `CampaignOptions.max_parallel` is therefore operationally inert despite the surrounding DAG, Herdr, checkpoint, and resource-management complexity.

Before an attempt, `CampaignBuilder._checkpoint_workspace` copies and fsyncs the entire workspace, state is persisted as running, `src/campaign_builder/features.py::FeatureRegistry.feature` resolves the feature, and the executor receives unversioned `FeatureContext` and returns `FeatureResult`. `CampaignBuilder._consume` records status, summary, artifacts, retry targets, and required changes. Success retains workspace mutation; failure or revision restores the checkpoint and invokes `_reset_stages` or `_route_retry`. Those paths compute affected descendants and clear state differently, so invalidation, handoff cleanup, promoted-output cleanup, retry accounting, and resource allocation do not form one reducer.

Build readiness adds a global handoff gate: the producer must have succeeded, the handoff must be approved, and its current SHA-256 must equal the approved digest. `src/campaign_builder/runtime.py::approve_handoff` binds approval to the current digest, but the approval path does not perform the full retained-state/config/evidence validation used by resume.

### Feature and execution plane

`src/campaign_builder/features.py` owns strict feature-config parsing and executor adapters for `agent`, `command`, `lean_contract`, `claim_ledger`, `semantic_contract`, and `artifact_promote`. Artifact exchange is path- and naming-based through `FeatureContext`, `FeatureResult`, command receipts, `retry_targets`, and `required_changes`; consumers may infer type from basename, schema field, or artifact count.

Agent attempts use `src/campaign_builder/agent_runner.py::execute`: parse an exact-key but unversioned request, write a running receipt, construct OMP arguments, optionally invoke `src/campaign_builder/sandbox.py::prepare_sandbox`, register ownership, pump/redact streams, poll deadline and workspace size, retry blank exit-zero output in the mutated workspace, run `sandbox.py::remove_secret_bearing_files`, and publish terminal output/log/receipt data. Existing output is not necessarily removed after final failure or blank output.

General subprocesses use `src/campaign_builder/command.py::run_captured_command`, which separately performs sandbox preparation, registration, launch in a new session, bounded stream pumping, timeout/workspace polling, group or unit termination, secret cleanup, and `CapturedCommand` construction. Different grace periods, hashes, thread cleanup, exit normalization, and retry behavior mean the two supervisors are not equivalent implementations of one contract.

`src/campaign_builder/sandbox.py::prepare_sandbox` filters the environment even when unsandboxed and, when sandboxed, compiles systemd cgroup properties plus bubblewrap mounts/namespaces. `terminate_sandbox`, `terminate_sandbox_unit`, `workspace_size_exceeds`, `secret_values`, and `redact_text` provide cleanup and policy helpers, but parsed policy is not backend enforcement evidence. Support depends on Linux, `/proc`, user systemd, and `bwrap`.

Resource recovery is split. `src/campaign_builder/process_registry.py` stores `RegisteredRunUnit` and `RegisteredRunProcess` records under runtime subdirectories; `src/campaign_builder/processes.py` stores controller and Herdr creation/workspace records directly under another runtime-root layout and exposes `terminate_run_processes` and `stop_all_campaigns`. `src/campaign_builder/herdr.py::HerdrClient` invokes the external CLI through `command.py::run_captured_command`; its workspace operations use loose JSON result envelopes, and `create_workspace` places environment values in argv.

### Evidence, verification, and outcome plane

`src/campaign_builder/artifacts.py::atomic_write_text` uses a same-directory temporary file, file flush/fsync, `os.replace`, and parent-directory fsync; `atomic_write_json` adds deterministic JSON formatting. `digest_file` and `digest_tree` produce SHA-256 descriptors. `verify_evidence_index` validates exact evidence-v1 keys, unique paths, regular non-symlink files, per-file hash/size, and equality between the indexed set and retained non-workspace files, subject to named controller exclusions in `verify_evidence_for_repair`.

`src/campaign_builder/journal.py::append_state_snapshot` locks, verifies the complete JSONL chain, truncates only an unterminated tail, and appends a canonical full-state snapshot with sequence and predecessor hash. `load_state_snapshot` also takes an exclusive lock, may truncate a tail, verifies the full chain, and returns the latest mutable state mapping. `src/campaign_builder/runtime.py::_save_state` writes journal before the `state.json` projection, but readers are not uniformly journal-backed.

Claims use `src/campaign_builder/claims.py::ClaimProposalBundle.load`, `ClaimVerdictBundle.load`, and `build_claim_ledger`. The proposal DAG, exact verifier/adjudicator coverage, verdict consistency, dependency blocking, required-target projection, and deterministic campaign-scoped claim IDs are implemented; mathematical truth, verifier independence, and evidence provenance remain external. Semantic review uses `src/campaign_builder/semantic.py::SemanticReview.load`, whose relation vocabulary and issue consistency are strict but whose records are not bound to campaign, target, source, Lean declaration, or toolchain identity.

Formal checking uses `src/campaign_builder/lean.py::run_proof_gate`, `scan_lean_sources`, `render_contract`, `GateCommandReceipt`, `AxiomReport`, and `ProofGateResult`. The visible command sequence includes `lake build`, `lake env which lean`, `lake env lean ...`, and an absolute Lean executable with `--run`. This is a distinct deterministic proof lane, but the supplied operations record identifies unresolved trust issues around shared compiler output, truncation, a second project-controlled `lake env`, and absence of one controller-authenticated complete report bound to the exact declaration and rendered contract.

`src/campaign_builder/inspection.py::inspect_claim_ledgers` and `inspect_assurance` derive reports from `state.json`, `configuration/features.json`, `configuration/resolved.json`, claim ledgers, and `verify_evidence_index`. They do not reconcile the state projection to `events.jsonl`, require successful ledger producers in every closure path, deeply recompute all ledger semantics, or pair Lean and semantic artifacts by common target/source/declaration/toolchain digests. `src/campaign_builder/metrics.py::collect_compute_metrics` and `write_compute_ledger` derive compute-ledger v1 from discovered request/receipt/output files, the caller-supplied current `OrchestrationPlan`, current `ProjectSpec`, and optional live `CampaignOutcome`; malformed requests may be silently skipped.

### Outer lifecycle and publication

`src/campaign_builder/regime.py::RegimeRunner` performs research gate, research/planning, frozen campaign generation, `CampaignBuilder` execution, model outcome assessment, knowledge/research publication, and successor-strategy choice. `src/campaign_builder/autonomy.py::AutonomyRunner` adds repeated regime campaigns and post-model deterministic build/type/axiom/verification commands; this is why benchmark execution through `src/campaign_builder/benchmark.py::run_benchmark_suite`, which runs `RegimeRunner` rather than `AutonomyRunner`, cannot be equated with autonomy’s kernel-checked success contract.

`src/campaign_builder/publication.py::plan_publication` verifies complete state/evidence/config/source, computes a canonical approval digest, and acquires locks in campaign-then-destination order; it is not read-only because it invokes `_recover_pending_publication`. `publish_campaign` recomputes the plan, stages exact bytes, writes `publication.pending.json`, swaps destination and backup, writes terminal receipts, fsyncs, and refreshes evidence. The prepared-before-swap exception path can remove staging while leaving a pending receipt that recovery requires.

`src/campaign_builder/cli.py::main` exposes the v1 command surface and fragmented exit behavior; several branches read `state.json` directly. `src/campaign_builder/terminal_status.py::PaneHeartbeat`, `RoleStatus`, and `ControllerDashboard` are correctly non-durable presentation constructs. `src/campaign_builder/__main__.py` is correctly a logic-free `SystemExit(main())` shim, while `src/campaign_builder/__init__.py` is an eager public facade whose exports require consumer inventory before removal.

## 4. What v1 got right

- **Strict compatibility boundaries are valuable.** `src/campaign_builder/config.py::CampaignSpec.load`, `src/campaign_builder/project.py::ProjectSpec.load`, model `.parse` methods in `src/campaign_builder/models.py`, handoff loaders in `src/campaign_builder/handoff.py`, claim loaders in `src/campaign_builder/claims.py`, and `src/campaign_builder/semantic.py::SemanticReview.load` generally use exact-key, fail-closed parsing. V2 should preserve their exact behavior only in read-only adapters and retain strict native parsing with explicit migrations.

- **The graph invariants are substantive.** `src/campaign_builder/config.py::_validate_stages` and `CampaignSpec.stage_order` enforce unique known stage IDs, acyclicity, deterministic lexicographic readiness, the research/build edge rule, and handoff producer requirements. `src/campaign_builder/models.py::OrchestrationPlan.parse` adds task/obligation/capability references, phase ordering, continuation-gate ancestry, and caller task/parallel bounds. `src/campaign_builder/claims.py::ClaimProposalBundle.topological_order` and `build_claim_ledger` preserve deterministic dependency closure. V2 should centralize these behaviors in one bounded DAG primitive without changing v1 adapter acceptance.

- **Required/failure consistency is explicit.** `src/campaign_builder/config.py::StageSpec` and `src/campaign_builder/models.py::PlanTask` both require `continue` with `required=false` and `abort|replan|restart` with `required=true`. This is a reusable policy primitive even though it is duplicated.

- **Verified input provenance has meaningful pins.** `src/campaign_builder/config.py::CampaignInput` requires `artifact`, expected campaign ID, expected run ID, and lowercase SHA-256 for `verified_artifact`, forbids those fields for other strategies, and forbids excludes for verified artifacts. `src/campaign_builder/runtime.py` further checks source campaign/run/status/evidence identity and verifies copied file hashes and sizes. V2 should preserve the semantic pins while replacing pathname traversal with immutable capabilities.

- **Atomic publication mechanics are directionally correct.** `src/campaign_builder/artifacts.py::atomic_write_text` and `atomic_write_json` use same-directory staging, file fsync, `os.replace`, parent fsync, and deterministic JSON. `src/campaign_builder/publication.py::PublicationPlan.canonical_dict`, `plan_publication`, and `publish_campaign` preserve fresh digest approval, run-before-destination lock ordering, staged verification, backup/rollback intent, and directory durability. These mechanics should survive behind a corrected transaction reducer rather than be discarded.

- **Evidence exact-set completeness is stronger than a loose artifact list.** `src/campaign_builder/artifacts.py::verify_evidence_index` verifies regular non-symlink final files, observed hash and size, unique indexed paths, and equality with the retained non-workspace set. V2 should retain this as a self-consistency proof while binding it to a stable snapshot, authoritative state sequence/digest, campaign/run identity, and an explicit trust label.

- **The journal has useful offline-audit properties.** `src/campaign_builder/journal.py` enforces physical sequence, canonical record hashing without the `sha256` field, `previous_sha256` linkage, fatal malformed complete records, and repair only for an unterminated tail. V2 should keep those audit semantics while moving full-chain verification off the append hot path and making `state.json` a digest-bound projection only.

- **Handoff approval is digest-bound.** `src/campaign_builder/runtime.py::approve_handoff` and build scheduling reject a handoff whose current SHA-256 differs from the approved digest. The v2 approval service should retain this invariant and add full bundle validation, campaign/run/producer/consumer binding, fd-confined reads, and approver-policy provenance.

- **Claims preserve disagreement rather than hiding it.** `src/campaign_builder/claims.py::ClaimVerdictBundle.load` requires exact proposal coverage; accepted verdicts cannot carry critical errors/gaps, rejected verdicts must carry a reason, and `build_claim_ledger` applies deterministic dependency blocking and governing-verdict rules. Explicit verifier disagreement/adjudication is a sound concept for v2 even though identity, independence, and evidence bindings need replacement.

- **Semantic relations are typed and internally constrained.** `src/campaign_builder/semantic.py::SemanticRelation` limits values to `equivalent`, `formal_stronger`, `formal_weaker`, `conditional_fragment`, `mismatch`, and `unclear`; `SemanticReview.load` requires no issues for `equivalent` and at least one issue otherwise. V2 should preserve the taxonomy while adding round-trip serialization and frozen target/source/declaration/toolchain bindings.

- **Formal verification is kept separate from model prose.** `src/campaign_builder/lean.py::run_proof_gate`, `AxiomReport`, and `ProofGateResult`; claim aggregation in `src/campaign_builder/claims.py`; semantic review in `src/campaign_builder/semantic.py`; and post-solve checks in `src/campaign_builder/autonomy.py::AutonomyRunner` express layered assurance rather than treating a model answer as proof. The operations record’s continuation-gate `stop` in run `20260824T205221Z-b63aa6` also demonstrates that fail-closed termination can be a successful assurance action without being a mathematical success.

- **Sandbox policy aims at defense in depth.** `src/campaign_builder/sandbox.py::prepare_sandbox` combines deterministic environment filtering, cgroup properties, bubblewrap mount/namespace restrictions, executable visibility, and network policy. The invariant is worth retaining, but native v2 must record backend capability preflight and enforcement attestations rather than infer enforcement from parsed configuration.

- **Process identity contains more than a PID.** `src/campaign_builder/process_registry.py::RegisteredRunProcess` and the stated execution invariant include PID, start time, process group, and session; supervisors use new sessions and intend surviving descendants to remain recoverable. V2 should preserve that identity model in one transactional resource ledger with boot ID, generation/nonce, lifecycle state, and CAS reconciliation.

- **Typed facades and non-durable presentation are good boundaries.** `src/campaign_builder/herdr.py::HerdrClient` is preferable to scattered direct CLI calls; `src/campaign_builder/terminal_status.py` correctly keeps heartbeat/dashboard state outside durable evidence; `src/campaign_builder/__main__.py` correctly contains no business logic; `src/campaign_builder/__init__.py` provides an explicit facade. V2 should replace their internals without casually deleting their public concepts.

- **The operational record correctly exposes status non-equivalence.** Run `20260824T091317Z-1cbb51` was operationally campaign-complete while its mathematical outcome remained `unsolved`; run `20260824T205221Z-b63aa6` stopped at a gate; the retained implementation record remains `ASSURANCE_NOT_RUN`. V2 should encode these as orthogonal fields rather than collapse them into `complete`, `success`, or `PASS`.

## 5. What v1 overbuilt or coupled badly

- **The controller monolith has no trustworthy refactor seam.** `src/campaign_builder/runtime.py::CampaignBuilder` owns run creation, locks, state validation, journal projection, scheduling, workspace copies, retry routing, evidence refresh, approval, process/Herdr recovery, replay, provenance import, and public status. Native v2 must split these into a store, pure reducer, scheduler, workspace transaction, evidence service, resource manager, approval service, importer, and replay service; the v1 class should remain a read-only/parity reference, not the base class for v2.

- **The feature layer mixes execution with mathematical policy.** `src/campaign_builder/features.py::FeatureRegistry`, `FeatureContext`, and `FeatureResult` join config parsing, prompt/request protocol, mutable workspace behavior, command and Lean receipts, claim/semantic closure, promotion, retries, and economics. The path-based, unversioned boundary permits artifact-type inference by basename, schema fields, or cardinality. V2 feature envelopes need explicit config/result/receipt schema IDs, implementation digests, capabilities, typed immutable inputs/outputs, and separate deterministic closure policy.

- **Execution supervision is duplicated and already divergent.** `src/campaign_builder/agent_runner.py::execute` and `src/campaign_builder/command.py::run_captured_command` disagree on deadline handling, grace periods, stream/digest semantics, retry behavior, cleanup, and terminal states while sharing private helpers. V2 needs exactly one `ExecutionSupervisor.run` lifecycle: validate capabilities, persist launch intent, prepare sandbox, spawn and bind identity, drain/hash byte streams, enforce deadline/quotas, terminate/reap, reconcile cleanup/resources, and atomically publish attempt artifacts. No pump may outlive return.

- **Resource authority is fragmented.** `src/campaign_builder/process_registry.py` separates process and transient-unit schema-v1 records; `src/campaign_builder/processes.py` adds incompatible controller and Herdr records; `src/campaign_builder/herdr.py::HerdrClient` has no durable child registration of its own. Duplicated `/proc` parsing, liveness, group logic, corruption skipping, and unlocked deletion make `processes.py::stop_all_campaigns` a best-effort sequence rather than a resumable saga. V2 needs one transactional `ResourceLedger` covering controller, process, unit, and Herdr resources.

- **Authority is split across journal, projection, and evidence.** `src/campaign_builder/journal.py` calls `events.jsonl` authoritative; `src/campaign_builder/runtime.py::_save_state` writes journal before `state.json`; `src/campaign_builder/inspection.py::inspect_assurance`, CLI branches in `src/campaign_builder/cli.py`, and shutdown/status paths nevertheless trust `state.json`. `src/campaign_builder/artifacts.py::verify_evidence_index` can bless both files without proving they represent the same state. V2 requires one validated commit head and a rebuildable `StateProjectionV2` carrying source sequence and digest.

- **Schema versioning is overloaded and unsafe.** `src/campaign_builder/config.py` labels incompatible campaign source TOML and resolved JSON as version 1; project, evidence, journal, registries, publication, compute, and many handoffs also use unrelated version-1 identities; `src/campaign_builder/models.py` mixes plan v3 and outcome v2; `src/campaign_builder/handoff.py::ReviewDecision` and agent requests are unversioned. V2 needs unique `artifact_type` plus version and explicit migration; it must never dispatch by filename or shallow field presence.

- **Source/resolved configuration drift weakens resume.** `src/campaign_builder/config.py::CampaignSpec.to_resolved_dict` emits effective values, while `CampaignSpec.load_resolved` allows missing execution/security sections to regain current defaults and omits several source, path, uniqueness, symlink, and exclude checks. `src/campaign_builder/project.py::ProjectSpec.with_compute_profile` applies overrides without revalidating all effective aggregate limits or retaining profile provenance. Native v2 needs separate source manifest, effective configuration, resolved project snapshot, and run snapshot schemas with all defaults materialized.

- **Shared primitives are repeatedly reimplemented.** Identifier and path parsing differ among `src/campaign_builder/config.py`, `project.py`, `models.py`, `claims.py`, and `handoff.py`; DAG and ancestry logic differ among campaign stages, plan tasks, claim proposals, ledger validation, and outcome promotion checks; `_fsync_directory` is duplicated in `src/campaign_builder/artifacts.py` and `journal.py`; object/key helpers recur throughout claims, semantics, inspection, metrics, and contracts. V2 should centralize bounded identifiers, paths, strict scalar parsing, deterministic encoding, DAG order/ancestry, and safe filesystem primitives while preserving v1 adapter quirks.

- **Mutable pathnames stand in for artifact identity.** `src/campaign_builder/runtime.py` and `src/campaign_builder/features.py` exchange workspace-relative strings, while `workspace/` is excluded from general `evidence.json` completeness. Successful source/output mutation can therefore escape broad resume detection, artifact references can become stale, and basename ambiguity affects consumers. V2 dependencies must use immutable content-addressed `ArtifactRefV2` values bound to producer run/stage/attempt and schema type.

- **Replay violates its claimed boundary.** `src/campaign_builder/runtime.py::replay_verifier_command` verifies selected declared inputs and sandbox metadata but executes against the live canonical completed workspace, permits undeclared persistent mutations, and then adds replay evidence. Native replay must use a disposable immutable read-only snapshot with declared output-only writes and must not rewrite the certified root.

- **Workspace rollback is expensive and crash-unsafe.** `src/campaign_builder/runtime.py::_checkpoint_workspace` full-copies and fsyncs the workspace before every attempt; `_restore_checkpoint` removes the live workspace before `copytree`. This amplifies I/O and permits a missing or partial live workspace after interruption, ENOSPC, or copy failure. V2 needs immutable roots and atomic root-pointer commit/discard or equivalent crash-safe snapshot transactions.

- **Repeated whole-tree work is overbuilt.** `src/campaign_builder/journal.py::append_state_snapshot` replays a growing chain of cumulative full states; runtime checkpoints copy whole workspaces; `src/campaign_builder/artifacts.py` evidence refreshes rehash broad retained trees; supervisors call `src/campaign_builder/sandbox.py::workspace_size_exceeds` on short polling intervals; executable/manifests and secret-bearing trees are repeatedly scanned; claim evidence is repeated per claim. The operations record confirms large no-output and timeout waste, including approximately 3601.921 seconds for `scalar_checker_pilot`, 3007.963 seconds before retry for `source_reconstruction`, and 3002.488 seconds on an exit-zero/no-output `source_carrier_producer` invocation. V2 storage and execution must be incremental and checkpoint novel changes, without treating wall time as CPU/token/billed compute.

- **Retry and invalidation are not one state transition.** `src/campaign_builder/runtime.py::_reset_stages` and `_route_retry` clear different fields, invalidate handoffs and promoted outputs through special cases, and can route work to an agent lacking a preallocated Herdr pane. Attempt budgets and exhausted-stage behavior are not uniformly encoded. V2 needs one deterministic dependency-derived invalidation operation with durable retry intent before deletion, artifact tombstones, explicit cause/ordinal/budget, and lazy idempotent resource acquisition.

- **Handoff validation has a parse/hash race and a split registry.** `src/campaign_builder/handoff.py::digest_handoff` parses a path and then rereads it to hash, so the returned digest may cover different bytes; `handoff_prompt_contract` and `digest_handoff` independently dispatch the same schema IDs; validators live in `handoff.py`, `claims.py`, and `semantic.py`; `src/campaign_builder/config.py::HandoffSpec.schema` does not resolve registration at manifest load. V2 must read bounded bytes once and have one registration own schema identity, parser, prompt fragment, migrations, required bindings, and digest domain.

- **Secret handling is split and semantically unstable.** `src/campaign_builder/config.py::_reject_inline_secret_values` performs ambient substring scanning; `src/campaign_builder/agent_runner.py` and `command.py` use different redaction/hash paths; `src/campaign_builder/sandbox.py::remove_secret_bearing_files` performs destructive workspace scanning. `command.py::CapturedCommand` digest meaning changes when secrets are configured. V2 must treat secret access as an opaque capability, define one stream publication/hash domain, and report quarantine or cleanup failure explicitly; substring scans may remain only as labeled lint.

- **Sandbox policy is mistaken for enforcement evidence.** `src/campaign_builder/sandbox.py::prepare_sandbox` compiles systemd and bubblewrap policy, but `systemctl is-active` classification, polling quotas, permission-mutating scans, and absent capability preflight prevent the parser result from proving live enforcement. V2 requests/results need versioned backend attestations and fail-closed capability checks.

- **Publication has a split crash protocol.** `src/campaign_builder/publication.py::plan_publication` mutates through recovery despite its name, and `publish_campaign` can leave `publication.pending.json` at `prepared` while cleanup deletes staging required by `_recover_pending_publication`. Path-based staging and destination checks also leave replacement races. V2 publication must be unavailable until an enumerated reducer and fault-injection coverage establish `planned → approved → prepared → swapped → commit_durable → cleaned`, with committed state only forward-repaired.

- **Mathematical closure is coupled through weak joins.** `src/campaign_builder/models.py::CampaignOutcome.parse` accepts a caller-supplied status; `CampaignOutcome.validate_against` does not derive solved from all mandatory obligations and evidence; `src/campaign_builder/inspection.py::inspect_assurance` can count unrelated Lean and semantic stages; `src/campaign_builder/claims.py::build_claim_ledger` cannot establish truth, independence, or provenance by itself. V2 must derive outcome from target-bound claim, Lean, semantic, obligation, provenance, approval, and evidence receipts; callers and models must not set `solved`.

- **Lean verification remains a distinct but incompletely sealed trust boundary.** `src/campaign_builder/lean.py::run_proof_gate` uses both Lake-mediated commands and a separately validated absolute Lean reader, compiles from mutable project state, and produces `ProofGateResult` schema v2 without all required source/toolchain/report bindings. The operations record’s open P1 items—shared stdout authority, truncation acceptance, second project-controlled `lake env`, and missing controller-authenticated complete report—must remain blockers, not be obscured by campaign-stage success.

- **Outer orchestration duplicates durable state machines.** `src/campaign_builder/regime.py::RegimeRunner`, `src/campaign_builder/autonomy.py::AutonomyRunner`, and `src/campaign_builder/benchmark.py::run_benchmark_suite` each add journal/projection/status/checkpoint/budget/decision behavior around `CampaignBuilder`. `benchmark.py` runs regime rather than autonomy while reporting outcome-oriented results. These layers should not enter the initial native core; later adapters must reference committed campaign/evidence IDs instead of embedding raw runtime dictionaries and path assumptions.

- **CLI and status semantics are fragmented.** `src/campaign_builder/cli.py` manually maps commands to exit codes and reads projections directly; `src/campaign_builder/runtime.py::campaign_status`, `campaign_status_exit_code`, regime statuses, autonomy statuses, benchmark outcomes, and model outcome labels overlap without one transition table. V2 must generate public status and exit mappings from the pure reducer and expose separate `code_status`, `controller_status`, `campaign_status`, `mathematical_status`, `assurance_status`, `publication_status`, and `incident_status`.

- **Assurance was overclaimed relative to retained execution.** Source-verified fixes and focused semantic tests exist, but the supplied operations record has no frozen integrated commit, full gate run, complete publication/recovery/process/secret/Lean fault matrices, or final independent audit with no open P0/P1. Future v2 agents must preserve `ASSURANCE_NOT_RUN` until those exact evidence classes exist; neither v1 campaign completion nor a process/audit exit code can upgrade that status.

## 6. Implementation and review incident history

**Evidence/status notation.** `H` = retained reviewer hypothesis or source concern without an explicit severity or final confirming reread; `P1` = explicitly recorded, source-located P1; `FIX-S` = a later current-tree reread verified the relevant symbol/control flow only; `FIX-E` = retained command output passed; `SUP` = finding withdrawn or replaced after a moving-tree reread. A landed edit or authored regression is **not** an executed fix. The retained record contains **no explicit P0 and no explicit P2**; it also contains no frozen-tree release gate from `Main`.

### Chronology

| Wave | Implementation/review event | Findings and disposition | Validation retained |
|---:|---|---|---|
| 1 | `Main` divided ownership across Python, manifests, and tests; workers were prohibited from running pytest, linters, formatters, mypy, Lake, or other gates. | The orchestration record ends at `eval(py) ⇒ pending`. This made later “complete” claims implementation claims only. | **None:** no final integration command, commit hash, or result. |
| 2 | `PublishAndStatus`, `VerifiedArtifactInput`, `EmptyOutputRetry`, `FrontierProfiles`, and `FrontierCampaigns` added publication/status, artifact import, blank-output retries, profiles, and two CMV campaign manifests. | Initial functionality was present, but publication authorization/provenance, runtime recovery, and campaign-DAG semantics had not been adversarially checked. Concurrent appends repeatedly collided in `tests/test_campaign_builder.py`. | Tests were authored but not run. |

The parent-reported 157-test and real-Lean smoke passes have no retained commands/output and are discarded as evidence. |

### Durable invariant catalog

| Domain | Required invariant | Retained implementation/evidence status |
|---|---|---|
| Authority | The hash-chained journal is authoritative; `state.json` is a replaceable projection. Status, stop, resume, retry, approval, strategy, publication recovery, and finalization start from the validated latest journal state while holding the run lock. | Partially source-implemented. INC-35 shows mirror repair still violates the legitimate journal-ahead case. |
| Run serialization | `campaign_run_lock(run_dir: Path)` uses exactly `.campaign.lock`, a no-follow open, nonblocking exclusive `fcntl.flock`, clear `CampaignRunError`, clean release, and spans mutation, execution, and recovery. Publication reuses this lock rather than defining another run lock. | `FIX-S`; no multiprocess/target-filesystem execution. |
| Evidence | A successful stage is indexed before dependents run. Resume verifies retained evidence before consuming dependencies, checkpoints, or terminal state and before reindexing; only exact controller/current-attempt paths may differ. | `verify_evidence_for_repair(..., permitted_paths=...)` and baselines are `FIX-S`; cross-recovery fault tests are absent. |
| Retry | Durable retry intent and `pending_output_cleanup` precede deletion; resume completes cleanup idempotently; producer reset invalidates approvals, promotions, downstream artifacts, and stale research. | Partially `FIX-S`; INC-34 remains open and no phase-by-phase crash run exists. |
| Handoffs | Every build is blocked on the configured approved handoff regardless of ancestry. Each attempt removes a previous regular/symlink handoff after inventory and before invocation, rejects special entries, and creates a fresh valid handoff. | `FIX-S`; `test_retry_requires_handoff_created_by_current_attempt` not run. |
| Verified artifacts | `expected_campaign_id`, `expected_run_id`, and lowercase `evidence_sha256` are jointly required only for `verified_artifact`; raw evidence is hashed before trust; evidence/state/directory identities match; imported files are nonempty, indexed regular files under a safe prefix. | Pins and checks are `FIX-S`; pin-mismatch/tamper/legacy tests not run. |
| Publication authorization | Planning is non-mutating. Canonical plan and approval digest bind campaign, run, evidence, source, root, destination, preserve type/policy, and every artifact; approval is exact lowercase SHA-256; staged bytes are reverified immediately before swap. | `PublishSpec.root`, `publish-plan`, canonical digest, and staged verification are `FIX-S`; canonical framing is not recoverable from the history. |
| Publication filesystem | Destination remains under configured root; traversal, overlap, symlink boundaries, and special preserved entries fail closed; one canonical destination lock serializes competing runs; preserve behavior is plan-bound. | `FIX-S`; alias locking, hardlinks, and adversarial filesystem behavior unexecuted. |
| Publication durability | Data and required directories are fsynced before `prepared`; preserve/recovery moves are fsynced before phase advance; durable `commit_durable` is irreversible and forward-repaired; terminal receipt recovery repairs evidence idempotently. | Helpers/order source-verified; no marker/rename/fsync fault injection. |
| Process ownership | Canonical run ownership is durable before launch. Process records retain PID, start time, group/session identity; unit records retain unit and `systemctl`; unregister/outcomes use full identity, never PID alone. | Original PID-only defects are `FIX-S`/`SUP`; group/session provenance remains open under INC-20. |
| Shutdown | Units and authenticated owned process members/groups are stopped and verified inactive before unregister or checkpoint restore. Identity mismatch, `EPERM`, non-`ESRCH` pidfd failure, cleanup failure, or unverifiable liveness remains an error/survivor. | Source paths were reread; no fork/session/systemd/PID-reuse execution. |
| Sandbox, secrets, replay | Every subprocess class uses an explicit containment/environment contract. Secret non-retention is value-aware across config, argv, env, output, receipts, workspace, evidence, failures, timeouts, interruption, and inaccessible paths; replay binds executable/env/toolchain and preserves evidence. | Only static aggregate closure; alternate-key, cleanup, replay-fingerprint, and mutation matrices are absent. |
| Regime/meta | Finalization is serialized under the canonical run lock; planning inputs are frozen; strategy intent/artifacts/state form a recoverable transaction; meta-agents use disposable workspaces and exact controller promotion; receipt reuse binds request/input/policy identity. | Major paths are `FIX-S`; INC-34 and absent crash/race execution prevent closure. |
| Semantic/Lean | Substitutions are typed data. Success requires exactly one report for `config.declaration` with config/report/assessment agreement. Trusted facts cannot come from writable, forgeable, ambiguous, truncated, or project-controlled channels. | Declaration binding has the only focused `FIX-E`; trusted-root issues are `SUP`, but INC-32 and INC-33 remain open P1s. |
| Runner retry | Retry iff an invocation exits 0 with whitespace-only stdout; total invocations equal `empty_output_retries + 1`; timeout, nonzero, spawn error, and nonblank success are not retried; per-attempt evidence is deterministic and top-level receipts remain compatible. | Implemented but unexecuted; no reviewer established a concrete loop defect. |
| Campaign data | Required approval is a deliberate pause, immutable contracts are actual DAG gates, profiles remain in role/stage, source challenge uses research scope, repair targets a proof-producing stage, and `contracts/frontier.lean` retains exactly the two required declarations. | INC-26 remains unclosed; no parser/DAG/route run is retained. |
| Audit claims | Keep separate: reviewer hypothesis, source-confirmed defect, explicit severity, executed reproduction, landed edit, source-verified fix, focused pass, full-suite pass, and independent post-fix closure. | Enforced in this history. The reported 157-test and Lean-smoke passes, skipped calls, opaque yields, and `eval(py) ⇒ pending` are not validation. |

### Why the adversarial waves helped—and where they churned

The waves were productive because they applied **different trust models to the same integrated behavior**. `RuntimeReviewer` followed configuration through runtime, CLI, and publication and exposed cross-owner defects such as the non-ancestral gate at `campaign_runtime.py:333-338`, absent `.campaign.lock` serialization, stale handoffs, malformed retained state, publication TOCTOU, and missing `publish-plan` UX. `CampaignReviewer` inspected manifest DAG semantics rather than Python mechanics and uniquely found the required-approval/“one-command” contradiction at `problems/cmv-type-four-exclusion/campaign.toml:37-40`, the missing bridge gate, profile-role errors, and an invalid proof-repair route. Filesystem, hermetic-environment, and secret reviews then exercised malicious retained state, symlinks/special entries, replay mutation, cleanup failure, and alternate-key secret **values**, demonstrating why secret-key-name matching was not a sufficient containment model.

Crash reviews added another non-overlapping perspective: they enumerated durable boundaries—journal append versus `state.json` projection, retry intent versus deletion, Herdr create versus registration, publication `prepared`/swap/commit markers, fsync placement, process registration versus `Popen`, pidfd acquisition versus signaling, and evidence refresh versus resume consumption. Broader system/coherence reviews were valuable after scoped “no P0/P1” verdicts because they reached `regime.py`, semantic declaration identity, stop-all registry discovery, strategy transactions, and recovered-publication evidence. Thus `FinalSecurityReview` could be correct about `RuntimeMaxSec` and pre-launch unit ownership while `FinalSystemReview` and later post-fix audits still found P1s elsewhere.

Moving-tree rereads also prevented false persistence: auditors correctly withdrew mutable trusted-root, `audit_olean`, separate-module import, PID-keyed outcome, start-mismatch, reaped-leader `killpg`, and incomplete-registration findings once the relevant code changed. Those are recorded as `SUP`, not silently mixed with open incidents.

Churn came from the opposite conditions: no frozen commit; repeated concurrent appends to `tests/test_campaign_builder.py`; API churn around `SandboxInvocation` and `systemctl`; interrupted or policy-skipped tool calls; line-number drift; broad grep/diff/full-file rereads that generated little new evidence; and opaque/truncated `yield(result)` records that lost the exact `PublicationSecurity` payload, `RecoveryStateMap` schema, several severities, and some Lean/process attack sequences. Most importantly, orchestration repeatedly required workers to finish without validation. Regressions therefore remained design artifacts, and later reviewers had to infer correctness statically. The history completed multiple implement→review→patch cycles, but never the decisive freeze→focused execution/fault injection→full gates→independent post-fix audit cycle on one commit.

## 8. Canonical CMV mathematical state

> **Canonical status — UNRESOLVED.** The CMV strip-density conjecture is not proved by the retained artifact set. The strongest defensible terminal claim is **at most** an explicit-coordinate, modeled, complete-topological-frontier type-(iv) exclusion at the exact rational cutoff
> \[
> \lambda_0=1.2581840884=\frac{3145460221}{2500000000},
> \]
> subject to a clean, hash-bound Lean rebuild and axiom audit. It is neither an all-\(\lambda>1\) theorem nor a theorem over the source finite-perimeter/reduced-boundary class.

### 8.1 Truth-label discipline

- **FORMAL** means a declaration is present or reported in Lean source with the stated scope; unless accompanied by a current clean-build/type/axiom receipt, it does **not** mean independently kernel-audited.
- **DETERMINISTIC** means a fact follows from artifact inventory, exact syntax, rational-literal semantics, dependency structure, or elementary exact arithmetic.
- **SOURCE-DERIVED** means reconstructed from the CMV/Morgan source or source-oriented reports; the cited PDF passages still require direct independent audit.
- **INTERVAL-CERTIFIED** means supported by a retained directed-rounding or exact-rational certificate under its stated checker, ABI, branch, and coverage assumptions; it is not automatically a Lean theorem.
- **NUMERICAL** means high-precision evaluation, root finding, sampling, plots, or residual checks only.
- **CONDITIONAL** means the conclusion is valid only if all displayed source/model, analytic, or trust antecedents hold.
- **REJECTED** marks an invalid inference or quarantined method.
- **UNRESOLVED** marks a missing theorem, bridge, receipt, or domain case.

No current terminal declaration qualifies as **FORMAL and independently audited** under the strict receipt rule: the aggregate does not bind a clean checkout, exact source hashes, theorem-type dump, import root, and `#print axioms` output into one current verification receipt. Historical reports of successful builds are evidence about earlier snapshots, not a current dependency-closed audit.

### 8.2 Canonical mathematical contract and milestone separation

- **SOURCE-DERIVED — target semantics.** The density is
  \[
  f_\lambda(x,y)=\begin{cases}
  1,&|y|\le 1,\\
  \lambda,&|y|>1,
  \end{cases}
  \]
  so the interfaces \(y=\pm1\) have density **one**, not \(\lambda\). Perimeter comparisons are meaningful only for competitors of **exactly equal weighted area** under the same semantics. See `projects/cmv-strip-density/problem.md` and the source reconstruction in `projects/cmv-strip-density/knowledge/source_analysis/20260824T091317Z-1cbb51-source_reconstruction.{json,md}`.
- **UNRESOLVED — full target.** A source-faithful theorem must quantify over the actual CMV admissible finite-perimeter class and use the source weighted reduced-boundary/BV perimeter. No supplied theorem has this scope.
- **FORMAL — scalar milestone only.** The Morgan scalar analysis concerns \(x>0\), \(\theta\in(0,\pi)\), and the function \(g\); it does not itself quantify over planar competitors.
- **FORMAL — modeled-coordinate milestone only.** The coordinate geometry concerns explicit `FourArcCandidate`, cap/core, and replacement constructors, with weighted \(H^1\) on their complete topological frontiers.
- **CONDITIONAL — source implication.** A modeled exclusion implies a source exclusion only after source normalization, admissibility preservation, perimeter equivalence, and minimality-quantifier transfer.
- **UNRESOLVED — type-(iii).** Type-(iii)/E3 analysis, type-(iv)/E4 exclusion, and the final CMV classification are distinct propositions. There is no supplied Lean `TypeThreeAssembly` or equivalent branch-complete type-(iii) carrier.
- **UNRESOLVED — configured all-domain target.** `project.toml` names an aspirational `CMVConjecture.cmv_conjecture_3_12`, but `projects/cmv-strip-density/proof/CMVConjecture.lean` is absent from the supplied proof inventory and is not a Lake root. Configuration is not theorem evidence.

```text
MorgansArcFunction → GFunction → Minimiser → MinimumValue → Certificate
CMVGeometry → FrontierPerimeter → FourArcCandidate / CapReplacement
            → GeometricBridge → CandidateExclusion
```

The file/declaration is not supplied or checked; even this proposed signature is model-level, not the full source finite-perimeter classification. |

### 8.4 Corrected formulas, domains, and source facts

- **DETERMINISTIC.** A Lean decimal such as `(1.2581840884 : ℝ)` is the exact rational coercion \(3145460221/2500000000\), not floating-point data. This proves numeral semantics only, not the associated inequality or provenance.
- **SOURCE-DERIVED.** The corrected source-formula term is
  \[
  \lambda\alpha-\sin\alpha\cos\beta,
  \]
  not \(\lambda(\alpha-\sin\alpha\cos\beta)\). See `projects/cmv-strip-density/knowledge/analytic/20260824T205221Z-b63aa6-scalar_formula_producer.{json,md}`.
- **SOURCE-DERIVED.** The regular source domain is \(0<h<1\). The value \(h=1\) is a coordinate/formula closure point only; \(h\to0^+\) is a separate degeneration.
- **SOURCE-DERIVED.** At E3 \(h=1/2\), the geometry is the Lemma 3.8(iii) zero-segment/radius-two degeneration, not type (i). E3 has major, semicircular, and minor branches; E4 regular arcs are minor in the reconstructed normalization.
- **DETERMINISTIC.** Distinct domains must remain distinct: analytic \(x>0\); inverse angle \(\theta\in(0,\pi)\); original type-(iv) cap angle \(\alpha\in(0,\pi/2)\); replacement angle \(\theta\in(0,\pi)\), including major caps; source-regular \(h\in(0,1)\); modeled `FourArcCandidate` closure \(h=1\).
- **DETERMINISTIC.** The displayed chain \(y\mapsto y/2\mapsto y/4\mapsto y/8\mapsto y/16\) contains four halvings, not three.
- **DETERMINISTIC.** Euclidean perimeter uses `WithLp 2`, not the product/max metric.
- **SOURCE-DERIVED / published-unverified.** Relevant reported CMV locators include Proposition 2.13, Propositions 3.5–3.9, Lemma 3.8, equations (24)–(27), Conjecture 3.12, Theorem 3.16, and Remark 3.17 in `projects/cmv-strip-density/references/Canete2010.pdf`. The retained PDF hash is `0ee9b4787aa3c09f9a4ed05fb66563a820cd13596af401b76e7900e597711230`; the quotations, numbering, branch conventions, and journal/arXiv normalization remain **UNRESOLVED** pending direct audit.

### 8.5 Exact, conditional, interval, and numerical conclusions

- **FORMAL / pending receipt.** Reported scalar minimum:
  \[
  k:=3\cos\theta_*>0,\qquad g(x)\ge k\quad(x>0),\qquad g(x_*)=k.
  \]
- **DETERMINISTIC from the reported minimum, but not exported/audited as one interface.** For \(\lambda>0\),
  \[
  \forall x>0,\;\frac1\lambda<g(x)
  \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
  \lambda>\lambda_*:=\frac1{3\cos\theta_*}.
  \]
  At \(\lambda=\lambda_*\), equality occurs at \(x=x_*\); below it, the universal strict inequality fails at \(x_*\).
- **FORMAL / pending receipt.** `Certificate.lean` reportedly establishes the sufficient exact-rational corollary at \(\lambda_0=1.2581840884\), with `GeometricBridge.cap_replacement_strictly_improves` and `CandidateExclusion.cmv_type_four_not_minimizing` propagating it to the explicit modeled geometry.
- **REJECTED.** \(1.2581840884\) is not the exact sharp threshold. It is a rational cutoff reported strictly above \(\lambda_*\). The inclusion of equality in \(\lambda\ge\lambda_0\) is compatible with strict improvement because \(\lambda_0>\lambda_*\).
- **CONDITIONAL.** The promoted equal-area work claims exact-human fold topology, complete scalar root multiplicity, descending-root least-perimeter selection, and reduction to a one-variable gap \(\Gamma(\lambda)\); see `knowledge/analytic/20260824T091317Z-1cbb51-equal_area_pilot.{json,md}`. These are not Lean source-level carrier theorems.
- **CONDITIONAL.** `knowledge/analytic/20260824T091317Z-1cbb51-full_domain_proof.json` promotes \(\Gamma(\lambda)<0\) for every \(\lambda>1\) only as scalar evidence assembled from low-density, compact, and large-density arguments. It is not one kernel-checked theorem and does not imply source exclusion.
- **INTERVAL-CERTIFIED / external trust.** `knowledge/computation/20260824T091317Z-1cbb51-interval_pilot.{json,md}` reports directed-MPFR coverage exactly on \([17/16,3/2]\), with 127 adjacent terminal boxes and `global_proof_claimed=false`. Its force is conditional on the Python/ctypes/MPFR ABI, directed-rounding implementation, branch contracts, and replay integrity.
- **INTERVAL-CERTIFIED / bounded pilot only.** `runs/20260824T235503Z-841a3c/workspace/agents/computation/exact_certificate_pilot/certificate.json` contains one narrow exact-rational row and an undischarged singular-face contract. It explicitly leaves `(1/256,1/8)` uncovered and records `global_proof_claimed=false`; it proves no global subcutoff theorem.
- **NUMERICAL.** Approximate values of \(\theta_*\), \(k\), \(\lambda_*\), sampled residuals, `findroot`, mpmath checks, and plots are corroboration/falsification telemetry only. `projects/cmv-strip-density/proof/verify_certificate.py` is a nontrusted smoke test with incomplete coverage of the optimized Lean certificate.

### 8.6 External bridges that remain mandatory

1. **UNRESOLVED — source contract audit:** verify CMV/Morgan definitions, candidate taxonomy, theorem hypotheses, density/interface convention, branch choices, endpoint cases, and source perimeter semantics directly from `references/Canete2010.pdf`.
2. **UNRESOLVED — source-to-E4 normalization:** map every relevant regular source type-(iv) representative, modulo density-preserving isometry and null-set convention, to a unique explicit E4/`FourArcCandidate` with \(0<h<1\), preserving carrier, incidence, area, perimeter, admissibility, and regularity.
3. **UNRESOLVED — BV/reduced-boundary bridge:** prove finite perimeter of E3, E4, and replacement assemblies; identify reduced boundary with complete frontier \(H^1\)-a.e.; handle interface traces, joins, cracks, endpoint-null sets, and density traces; prove equality of weighted integrals.
4. **UNRESOLVED — source-faithful type-(iii):** construct all major/semicircular/minor E3 branches, including the corrected \(h=1/2\) transition, and prove exact area/perimeter formulas and source correspondence.
5. **UNRESOLVED — kernel all-domain scalar theorem:** replace the human+MPFR synthesis by one proof of \(\Gamma(\lambda)<0\) for every \(\lambda>1\), with explicit joins at \(17/16\) and \(3/2\), fold/root completeness, and all boundary cases.
6. **UNRESOLVED — scalar-to-competitor realization:** turn each selected equal-area descending E3 root into an actual source-admissible competitor of exactly equal weighted area and strictly lower source perimeter.
7. **UNRESOLVED — minimality transfer:** prove that minimization among all source finite-perimeter equal-area sets implies the internal modeled minimizer predicate; do not encode this as an assumption-shaped compatibility field or restrict the quantifier silently to circular constructors.
8. **UNRESOLVED — classification:** only after type-(iv), type-(iii), endpoint, existence, and source-classification premises are closed may CMV Theorem 3.16 or Conjecture 3.12 be invoked unconditionally.

### 8.7 Rejected and disproved approaches

- **REJECTED:** “CMV Conjecture 3.12 is proved,” “type-(iv) is excluded for every \(\lambda>1\),” or “the configured `CMVConjecture.cmv_conjecture_3_12` exists and is verified.”
- **REJECTED:** identifying type-(iii) with names containing `type_four`, `.general`, or E3 prose. No correspondence theorem exists.
- **REJECTED:** promoting internal `AdmissibleCompetitor` universality to all source finite-perimeter competitors.
- **REJECTED:** equating complete topological-frontier \(H^1\) with reduced-boundary/BV perimeter by terminology or regularity intuition.
- **REJECTED:** claiming strict universal cap improvement at \(\lambda=\lambda_*\); equality occurs at \((\lambda_*,x_*)\).
- **REJECTED:** claiming anything universal below \(1.2581840884\) from the current Lean candidate-exclusion path.
- **REJECTED:** `verify_full_domain.py` and any `PASS`/`EXACT` result derived from it. Adversarial tests showed that it accepted malformed or sign-reversed evidence; it is quarantined by `knowledge/analytic/20260824T091317Z-1cbb51-full_domain_proof.json`.
- **REJECTED:** fixed-core/fixed-junction redistribution as an exclusion mechanism. The retained result instead says the symmetric cap pair minimizes within that restricted class; see `knowledge/geometric/20260824T091317Z-1cbb51-geometric_variation_pilot.{json,md}`.
- **REJECTED:** a single numerical root or continuation path as complete equal-area evidence; retained probes exhibit two-root cases in which one root loses and the descending root wins.
- **REJECTED:** treating build-job counts, notebook prose, theorem names, `optimization.json`, index membership, process exit zero, or a static Markdown-only notebook as proof receipts.

### 8.8 Dependency-ordered closure chain

0. **DETERMINISTIC — freeze evidence:** pin repository commit, Lean toolchain, Mathlib manifest, Python, mpmath, MPFR/ABI, source PDF, generated certificates, and hashes.
1. **UNRESOLVED — verify current Lean baseline:** add one root importing every public target; clean-build it; dump exact theorem types/import DAG; scan for `sorry`, `admit`, custom axioms, unsafe/native substitution, and conclusion-shaped assumptions; capture `#print axioms`.
2. **DETERMINISTIC — normalize claim authority:** encode a typed claim DAG in which promotion JSON overrides rhetoric, audit overlays override producers, and modeled/source/conditional claims have distinct IDs.
3. **SOURCE-DERIVED — lock the source contract:** define the source competitor class, weighted volume, reduced-boundary perimeter, type-(iii)/(iv) predicates, domains, branches, and interface convention from audited source passages.
4. **FORMAL — canonicalize the scalar API:** one namespaced specification for segment area/length, `θOf`, `arc`, `g`, E3/E4 formulas, branch tags, dimensions, and guarded domains.
5. **FORMAL — package the inverse and scaling:** replace witness-centric inversion by an order/homeomorphism interface; prove continuity, differentiability, asymptotics, and general chord scaling \(x=A/L^2\) for \(L>0\).
6. **FORMAL — complete the sharp threshold API:** export `k`, `k>0`, `IsLeast`, unique argmin, exact attainment, and strict/equality/failure cases for \(\lambda\).
7. **FORMAL / INTERVAL-CERTIFIED — rebuild certificates:** generate exact proof objects replayed by Lean; retain compatibility theorem names and exact rational cutoffs; emit a witness/margin manifest.
8. **FORMAL — retain and audit explicit geometry:** preserve cap/core carriers, Euclidean `WithLp 2`, area formulas, frontier traces, major replacement branch, and modeled equal-area/perimeter identities.
9. **FORMAL — verify the topological-frontier bridge:** audit primitive pushforward-to-\(H^1\) theorems, overlap nullity, and constructor equality with `FrontierMeasure`.
10. **FORMAL — build the BV bridge:** prove finite perimeter, reduced-boundary identification, trace convention, and equality with source weighted perimeter.
11. **FORMAL — normalize source type-(iv):** enforce \(0<h<1\), separate \(h=1\) closure, and prove universal source-to-coordinate preservation.
12. **FORMAL — implement source type-(iii):** all E3 branches, corrected \(h=1/2\) degeneration, formulas, admissibility, and source normalization.
13. **FORMAL — equal-area topology:** prove folds, all roots, descending-root existence, second-root criterion, least-perimeter selection, and endpoint equality cases.
14. **FORMAL — all-domain scalar sign:** prove \(\Gamma<0\) for all \(\lambda>1\) with low/compact/large joins and uniform boundary handling.
15. **FORMAL — construct the source competitor:** instantiate the selected E3 root as an exactly equal-area source set with strictly lower reduced-boundary perimeter.
16. **FORMAL — transfer minimality quantifiers:** connect source minimization over all finite-perimeter competitors to the modeled contradiction interface without theorem-shaped assumptions.
17. **FORMAL — export separate terminal theorems:** sharp scalar criterion; modeled cutoff exclusion; conditional source cutoff exclusion; all-\(\lambda\) modeled exclusion; all-\(\lambda\) source type-(iv) exclusion; type-(iii) result; final CMV classification.
18. **DETERMINISTIC — close operations:** generate reports, theorem snippets, cutoff metadata, axiom manifests, and unresolved matrices from the claim DAG; point `project.toml` success only at a compiled, audited theorem of matching scope.

### 8.9 What compilation would—and would not—prove

A clean Lean build would establish only that the exact declarations elaborate and are accepted by the kernel relative to their imports and axioms. Even successful compilation does **not** prove source fidelity, correctness of CMV quotations, non-vacuity of hypotheses, completeness of `FourArcCandidate` or `AdmissibleCompetitor`, equivalence with the source finite-perimeter class, reduced-boundary semantics, type-(iii) coverage, minimizer existence, numerical prose, cutoff optimality, novelty, computability of `θOf`/`θstar`, absence of `sorryAx` in unqueried dependencies, or the full CMV conjecture. Those require the external bridges and receipts listed above.

## 9. Behavioral specification from tests and benchmarks

### 9.1 Status and scope of the evidence

This specification is behavioral, not an assertion about private implementation structure. The supplied audit identifies exact source files but not individual pytest symbols; therefore contracts are linked to files and invariant classes without inventing test names.

- **TESTED** — the supplied audit describes an exercised assertion with the stated scope.
- **IMPLIED** — required by configuration, prompts, or architecture, but not established by the exercised assertion.
- **REQUIRED-V2** — a normative contract needed to close an incident-derived gap.
- **DOES NOT PROVE** — an explicit limit on the inference that may be drawn from a passing test.

Every migrated test record **MUST** include: exact test symbol, source file, incident ID/class, invariant ID, `proves`, `does_not_prove`, real/fake boundary, platform and required capabilities, priority, owner/tier, schema versions, deterministic seed/profile, expected machine code, and linked acceptance gate. Historical private-helper ordering, prompt wording, exact role-directory names, incidental list/dict representations, and human diagnostic prose are not behavioral contracts unless separately declared as public compatibility surfaces.

Revalidate mutable facts at the last safe boundary. |

### 9.3 Test architecture and release gates

V2 verification is layered; a higher layer does not erase the need for a lower one.

1. **L0 — schema and pure contracts:** typed builders and JSON Schemas; strict keys/types/enums/bounds; canonical hashes; DAG and status algebra; arithmetic; stable machine codes and JSON paths.
2. **L1 — model-based state machines:** fake clock plus deterministic barriers; journal projection, retries, approvals, publication, workspaces, benchmark checkpoints, regression commits, source promotion; crash at every durable boundary and compare with a reference model.
3. **L2 — unprivileged subprocess integration:** installed CLI entry points, real local process trees, fake-agent executable protocols, sanitized environments, bounded stdio, timeout and registration behavior, atomic output, non-ASCII/space-bearing paths, and fresh-process replay.
4. **L3 — adversarial filesystems:** descriptor-relative/no-follow operations; symlink, hardlink, junction where supported, special file, traversal, case collision, parent swap, stat/read growth, permission, disk-full, decompression, preexisting-output, concurrent-writer, and rollback cases.
5. **L4 — privileged Linux containment:** procfs, pidfd, cgroup v2, systemd user units, mount/network namespaces, user-bus denial, resource limits, resistant descendants, PID reuse, EPERM, EMFILE/ENOSYS, and orphan ambiguity. Unsupported capabilities are reported explicitly rather than silently skipped.
6. **L5 — real toolchain and adapter oracles:** pinned Lean build/signature/axiom oracle, independent formal invocation where soundness is claimed, captured real Herdr compatibility fixture, and optional pinned TensorFlow CPU replay.
7. **L6 — end-to-end security/autonomy:** public lifecycle APIs, isolated benchmark runs, private adjudicators, tainted canaries, checkpoint/resume, false-closure traps, claims/semantic gates, publication, and retained provenance.
8. **L7 — mutation/fuzz/exploratory:** journal mutations, malformed/noisy parsers, redaction overlap corpora, path races, generated graphs, boundary configs, artifact tampering, removal of every mandatory certificate obligation, benchmark leakage mutations, and fake scenario coverage. Minimized failures become deterministic incident fixtures.

A release **MUST** satisfy all applicable P0 gates: schemas; process containment; durability and exactly-once convergence; preflight-before-mutation; evidence closure; publication transactionality; secrets/sandbox; Lean/semantic target binding; claims closure; regression safety; and benchmark isolation/adjudication. P1 gates cover orchestration, planner governance, and tool compatibility. The release report lists `passed`, `failed`, `unsupported`, `quarantined`, and `skipped_by_capability`; a skipped P0 is a release failure unless the corresponding platform/capability is explicitly outside the published support statement. NFS/distributed exactly-once behavior, non-Linux equivalents for Linux containment primitives, external-network testing, semantic novelty as a hard gate, and real OMP semantic quality remain out of scope unless explicitly added.

### 9.4 Benchmark truth, migration, and acceptance

The current `benchmarks/autonomy-calibration/validation/validate_cases.py` is a **fixture self-check**, not an autonomy calibration result. It TESTS matching ID-to-legacy-label maps, 12 unique expected IDs, the historical 7 solved / 2 blocked / 3 unsolved counts, selected arithmetic witnesses and source sentinels, literal Lean holes, and compilation of three colocated standalone canonical solutions. It does **not** consume agent runs, model/provider/version, seed, repetitions, latency, resources, scoring errors, retry/checkpoint behavior, evidence usage, repaired challenge projects, unchanged signatures, `lake build`, private-answer isolation, or complete prompt obligations. Its use of Python `assert` is unsafe under `python -O`; exact Lean stdout prose is version-coupled; duplicate manifest IDs and focused/project manifests are incompletely validated.

V2 benchmark semantics are authoritative as `status` plus `reason_code`:
