- preserve seven solved fixture intents;
- `redacted-mean` and `missing-table-entry`: `blocked / missing_evidence`;
- `dependent-system`: `underdetermined`;
- `odd-prime-conjecture` and `signed-cross-multiplication`: `refuted`;
- retain legacy `unsolved` only as a migration projection, never as the authoritative mathematical status;
- encode the primary/focused `missing-table-entry` transition difference with explicit override provenance and test both `continue` and `checkpoint` behavior.

Benchmark acceptance is **existential and obligation-based**: arithmetic accepts any certificate satisfying every typed mandatory obligation; Lean accepts any unchanged-signature, kernel-valid, policy-compliant proof. Canonical prose and answer files are documentation or private smoke witnesses, not sole accepted outputs. Acceptance requires all of the following:

1. Versioned strict schemas for suite, project, case, evidence, run, verifier, result, and policy transition; duplicate IDs and path/project mismatches are rejected.
2. Ordinary exceptions and structured per-case JSON, with identical invalid-fixture rejection under normal Python and `python -O`.
3. Validation of primary and focused manifests, every project manifest, path existence/containment, and executable transition overrides with rationale/provenance.
4. Private adjudication material physically outside agent reach under an explicit channel inventory covering files, names, symlink targets, parent paths, environment, argv, prompts, directives, logs, network, and post-run outputs; tainted canaries demonstrate noninterference.
5. One typed check or declared rubric item for every prompt obligation; removal of any mandatory witness causes rejection.
6. Deterministic preflight for arithmetic, rank, residue, sign, source, declaration, signature, and other decidable obligations before model use.
7. Actual run artifacts determine outcomes; fixture answers never stand in for executions. Empirical metadata, confusion matrices, false-positive/false-negative rates, latency, retries, checkpoints, resources, and provider/model identity are stored separately from fixture validation.
8. Lean cases build the actual staged challenge with pinned tooling, exact declaration/signature and axiom checks, retained commands/environment/output digests, and private adjudication fixtures.
9. False closure is a hard veto: process success, schema validity, lane separation, compilation, semantic review, mathematical disposition, and request fulfillment remain separate fields.
10. Release invariants: zero unreceipted invocations, zero execution from a null/unaccepted plan, zero fail-open continuation, zero false closures, zero unexplained model identities, and no skipped applicable P0 benchmark contract.

## 10. Model-routing policy

### 10.1 Authority and invariants

Routing is deterministic control-plane policy. Models may propose content; they **MUST NOT** choose their own tier, provider, tools, capabilities, timeout, retry count, parallelism, status, closure, continuation, or publication authority. Schema parsing, canonicalization, DAG/claim closure, budgets, capability grants, scheduling, retries, hashing, state transitions, success computation, receipt validation, exact arithmetic, and formal replay remain deterministic.

Evidence classes remain separate: `controller_status`, `execution_status`, `artifact_status`, `verification_status`, `semantic_status`, `scientific_status`, `gate_decision`, and `outcome_reason`. `process_succeeded` does not imply `artifact_valid`; `artifact_valid` does not imply `claim_verified`; `claim_verified` does not imply semantic equivalence; and none alone implies `campaign_solved` or publication approval. A deterministic counterexample, exact checker, or Lean kernel rejection outranks every model verdict. A model cannot upgrade or waive such a result.

“Playbook-first” has two parts:

- **Deterministic code** executes all fully algorithmic control and verification steps.
- **Luna** executes only the bounded language-bearing steps of an already selected, typed, deterministic playbook.

Terra maintains and implements ordinary reasoning-intensive work under known methods. Sol is the named mathematical Planner and frontier escalation/adjudication tier. Humans retain narrow nondelegable authority.

### 10.2 Exact tier assignment

| Route | Necessary and sufficient eligibility | Authorized work | Explicit prohibitions | Terminal output |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Deterministic** | A known algorithm or policy has typed inputs and a machine-checkable oracle, or the task controls identity, security, budgets, capabilities, state, routing, closure, or acceptance. | Schema/config/project parsing; canonicalization; path confinement; hashes; journal projection; DAG/status/claim closure; capability compilation; budget and scheduler decisions; command supervision policy; exact/numeric tools; Lean build/signature/axiom verification; benchmark adjudication; artifact verification; retry/state transitions; routing itself. | No prose inference where a machine field exists; no fallback to a model for malformed data, missing dependencies, infrastructure faults, or failed security checks. | Typed success or typed failure with stable code/path and receipts. |
| **Luna** | All hard validation passes; exactly one registered playbook matches; remaining work is bounded extraction, rendering, normalization, protocol adaptation, or elementary exposition; output has an exact schema; no output field controls truth, security, identity, paths, capabilities, budget, status, or continuation. | Execute known/deterministic language playbooks; CLI/help adapters; structured extraction from retained sources; report rendering from adjudicated records; protocol-facade maintenance; low-risk fixture/docs normalization; elementary explanation after a deterministic certificate exists. | No route/tool/budget/status/claim/continuation choice; no unresolved mathematics; no security authority; no semantic auto-repair. | Schema-valid artifact accepted by deterministic validation. |
| **Terra** | A registered playbook requires substantive cross-artifact or code reasoning, ordinary mathematical proposal/checking, routine formal repair, or implementation/maintenance of an execution kernel; no demonstrated frontier gap exists. | Implement and maintain ordinary reasoning systems: subprocess supervisor, process registry/resource ledger, journal/publication/sandbox code under an approved design, benchmark-integrity work, routine Lean proof repair, mathematical proposals and clean-room verification, ordinary semantic review as nonauthoritative input. | No controller-policy redesign; no self-authorization; no target changes; no claim closure; no assumption that difficult engineering is Sol work. Process containment, publication, schemas, schedulers, and sandbox engineering remain Terra/deterministic/human-security work, not Sol work. | Evidence-bearing proposal, patch, proof attempt, audit, or typed limitation under the assigned contract. |
| **Sol** | A deterministic frontier or critical-adjudication gate passes and a valid admission receipt exists. | **Planner:** obligation decomposition and distinct mathematical methods for a genuinely unresolved campaign. **Frontier invention:** mandatory frontier obligation after cheaper playbooks fail. **Critical adjudication:** conflict on a mandatory mathematical claim when deterministic/formal replay cannot decide and false acceptance could close or materially redirect the primary target. | No parsing, scheduler, process, publication, sandbox, routine implementation, ordinary Lean repair, operational planning, tool authorization, target mutation, success determination, continuation authorization, or publication approval. Sol prose never overrides a kernel/counterexample or computes closure. | Typed `plan`, `infeasible`, `contract_invalid`, frontier attempt, or one-verdict-per-claim adjudication. |
| **Human** | The decision is authoritative, nonmathematical, exceptional, or unsafe to delegate. | Freeze/approve problem and success contracts; authoritative informal-to-formal equivalence; security threat-model and capability exceptions; destructive publication approval/recovery; regime/autonomy policy amendments; source/license exceptions; exceptional authenticity/corruption adjudication; explicit budget amendments. | No waiver of kernel failure, deterministic refutation, missing mandatory evidence, or fabricated receipts. Humans do not perform routine controller transitions. | Digest-bound decision, rationale, scope, expiry if applicable, and independent deterministic validation. |
| **Formal/exact verifier** | A verifier contract exists. This is an authority lane, not a model tier. | Lean kernel/build/axiom audit, exact arithmetic, certificate checker, schema validator, deterministic counterexample search, replay service. | Cannot be replaced by model consensus or diversity. | Reproducible verifier receipt that may reject any model tier. |

### 10.3 Deterministic routing algorithm

For each task or unresolved obligation, the controller performs the following sequence exactly:

1. **Freeze identity:** assign task and obligation IDs; hash target, success contract, sources, artifacts, policy, allowed axioms, and required output schema; record risk/impact and authorized capabilities.
2. **Validate preconditions:** reject malformed schemas, unsafe paths, stale evidence, missing mandatory sources, unauthorized capabilities, incompatible toolchains, exhausted budgets, and tampered inputs. These are terminal or deterministic-recovery conditions, not intelligence escalations.
3. **Match playbooks:** query the versioned capability/playbook registry by capability ID and typed preconditions, never by prompt keywords. Zero, one, or multiple matches is recorded. Multiple matches require deterministic policy disambiguation; models do not choose.
4. **Run cheap falsification:** exact arithmetic, bounded counterexample search, DAG/path checks, static Lean contract checks, retained-evidence replay, dependency/toolchain health probes, and prior-success reuse.
5. **Execute deterministic implementation** whenever an algorithmic implementation and oracle exist. No model is called for manifest parsing, path safety, status/closure, budget decisions, benchmark answer checking, or acceptance computation.
6. **Route to Luna** only for the bounded language step of the selected playbook and only if every Luna eligibility predicate passes.
7. **Route to Terra** when substantive ordinary code/mathematical/formal reasoning remains under a known method. Operational fields are controller-generated; Terra supplies content and requested capability needs only.
8. **Evaluate frontier eligibility** only for a mandatory unresolved mathematical obligation. Without a passing `frontier_eligible` or `critical_adjudication_eligible` receipt, invoking Sol is illegal.
9. **Instantiate Sol Planner** only if the unresolved campaign needs genuine mathematical decomposition/invention. Deterministic policy validates and compiles Sol’s typed mathematical plan; Sol does not schedule it.
10. **Require continuation:** expensive descendants require a signed/hash-bound deterministic gate naming pilot outputs, accepted/rejected/unknown claims, exact artifact hashes, remaining budget, and an explicit `continue`. Optional-gate failure, `failure_policy=continue`, process success, or model prose cannot authorize continuation.
11. **Compute closure deterministically** over the obligation DAG, preserving accepted independent evidence and rerunning only the affected obligation and descendants.
12. **Classify every failure before retry:** `contract_schema`, `infrastructure`, `resource`, `evidence_integrity`, `mathematical_refuted`, `mathematical_unresolved`, `semantic_unclear`, or `policy_approval`. Only `mathematical_unresolved` can contribute to frontier escalation; infrastructure, schema, sandbox, and integrity failures return to deterministic repair or terminate.

### 10.4 Objective escalation and stop triggers

#### Deterministic → Luna

All conditions are required:

1. hard validation and preflight pass;
2. exactly one approved playbook applies;
3. only bounded extraction/rendering/normalization remains;
4. exact output schema and deterministic validator exist;
5. no field affects truth, security, identity, path, capability, budget, status, or continuation.

Limit: **two Luna invocations**—initial plus one syntax/omission repair. A second invalid result becomes `MODEL_CONTRACT_FAILED`; malformed JSON, blank output, timeout, dependency failure, or sandbox failure never escalates for “more intelligence.”

#### Luna → Terra

Trigger only when either:

- deterministic classification identifies substantive cross-artifact/code reasoning or mathematical entailment; or
- a schema-valid Luna artifact fails a **substantive** deterministic verifier.

Do not trigger on syntax, blank output, timeout, spawn error, missing dependency, path denial, or sandbox failure. Terra receives the frozen artifact and verifier codes, not hidden Luna dialogue.

#### Terra retry

A retry is admitted only when the prior attempt has:

- a typed limitation code;
- a materially different method signature;
- a declared machine-checkable artifact expected from the new method; and
- remaining per-obligation and aggregate budget.

Whitespace, paraphrase, prompt-wrapper changes, timestamps, or a larger token budget are not novelty. Preserve accepted claims and rerun only implicated obligations. Limit: **two Terra producer methods plus one independent Terra verification** per obligation.

#### Terra → Sol frontier invention

All seven predicates are mandatory:

1. a mandatory primary-target obligation remains `UNRESOLVED_MATH`;
2. deterministic falsification and every applicable registered playbook are exhausted;
3. at least two materially distinct Terra attempts have valid evidence receipts, unless the approved success contract preclassifies the obligation as frontier;
4. an independently launched Terra auditor confirms a mathematical gap rather than infrastructure, schema, resource, source, or integrity failure;
5. no deterministic counterexample, exact checker, or kernel rejection settles the obligation;
6. remaining Sol and aggregate budgets are positive; and
7. expected impact is primary-target closure or removal of a blocking high-value uncertainty.

The controller then emits `frontier_eligible=true` with unresolved IDs, playbook-search result, failed method signatures, source-gap status, retained evidence digests, maximum Sol calls, and output schema. No receipt means no legal Sol call. Limit: **two Sol invention attempts**.

#### Sol Planner admission

Planner admission additionally requires an immutable packet containing the problem/success-contract hash, exact target and obligation registry, source/evidence inventory, playbook matches, failed methods, deterministic falsification results, frontier rationale, authorized capabilities, remaining budget, and typed plan schema. Sol may return only `plan`, `infeasible`, or `contract_invalid`.

A plan must preserve the primary target, cover all mandatory obligations, provide mathematically distinct method signatures and falsification pilots, identify evidence classes, and contain exactly one explicit continuation gate before expensive descendants. Deterministic validation checks IDs, DAG, reachability, target fidelity, gate semantics, capability catalog, evidence contracts, and budget ceilings. At most **one evidence-linked field-level Sol revision** is allowed; a local defect does not justify full regeneration. Descendants cannot schedule while `adopted_plan_digest` is null.

If every mandatory obligation maps to an approved template and no frontier gap exists, the controller compiles the plan without Sol. Luna or Terra may fill bounded task content, recorded as template execution rather than a Sol plan.

#### Terra/Sol → Sol adjudication

Allowed only when schema-valid, evidence-bearing producer and verifier artifacts conflict on a mandatory mathematical claim; deterministic/formal replay cannot decide; and erroneous acceptance could close or materially redirect the primary target. The adjudicator is a third identity, receives frozen artifacts and disagreement codes without producer dialogue, cannot rewrite the claim, and returns exactly one verdict per claim. Limit: **one Sol adjudication**, plus a second independent Sol only when deterministic policy labels impact `critical`.

#### Sol → human

Required for `UNCLEAR` informal-to-formal correspondence, target-changing source interpretation, security exceptions, destructive publication, contract amendments, source/license exceptions, authenticity/corruption disputes, or unresolved disagreement between two independent Sol judgments. Human review count and scope are explicit; any budget override requires a digest-bound amendment.

#### Immediate no-escalation outcomes

The following stop or block without a higher reasoning tier: deterministic refutation; exact required-declaration kernel failure; missing mandatory evidence; exhausted budget; tampered or untrusted artifact; target substitution; unauthorized capability; sandbox preflight failure; stale identity; failed evidence pin; or unverifiable process ownership. The typed result distinguishes `refuted`, `blocked`, `failed`, `verifier_error`, `budget_exhausted`, and `checkpoint`.

### 10.5 Producer/reviewer diversity and independence

- A reviewer is a separately launched principal with no producer conversation, hidden scratch state, writable canonical workspace, or unreviewed summary. It receives only frozen source/proposal artifacts and a typed rubric.
- Routine mathematical review requires **Terra producer + clean-room Terra verifier + deterministic closure**. Formal claims additionally require the formal/exact verifier; model diversity never substitutes for the kernel.
- High-impact review requires producer and verifier to differ in at least two of `{provider, model_family, checkpoint_or_version, organization}` where approved options exist. If only one provider is approved, use a different family/checkpoint plus clean-room context and retain an approved limitation.
- Sol adjudication is a third identity and cannot be the Sol Planner, producer, or prior verifier. It receives proposal/verdict digests, disagreement codes, and an evidence inventory.
- For high-impact semantic equivalence, the authoritative human reviewer is distinct from the artifact producer and contract author. Sol may supply only a nonauthoritative review.
- For high-impact security/publication changes, the human approver is distinct from the implementer; deterministic tests and transaction receipts remain mandatory.
- `independence_status` is mechanically computed as `satisfied`, `degraded_with_approved_exception`, or `failed` from retained identities and context lineage. Self-assertions such as “cold” or “independent” have no effect.
- Review is claim-complete: exactly one terminal verdict per proposal claim, every mandatory target covered, disagreements retained, and no verdict may repair statement, scope, assumptions, or quantifiers. Any change creates a new content hash and restarts review.
- Promotion requires a distinct, downstream, successful, audit-classified, evidence-bearing, target-bound verifier edge. Same-stage, same-context, failed, or evidence-free reviews cannot promote knowledge.

### 10.6 Required receipt fields

Every invocation, transition, review, and publication effect is represented by a versioned receipt. Required field groups are:

| Group | Required fields |
|---|---|
| **Envelope** | `artifact_type`, `schema_version`, `producer_version`, `campaign_id`, `run_id`, `stage_id`, `attempt_id`, `invocation_id`, sequence, prior/current event hash, creation time, monotonic timing anchor, anti-replay nonce, intended consumers, payload hash, signature/attestation. |
| **Routing** | Task/obligation IDs; selected route; task class; risk/impact; matched playbook ID/version; every eligibility predicate and value; rejected alternatives and reason codes; escalation-parent receipt; approver; policy and budget digest. |
| **Model identity** | Provider, endpoint, family/name, exact checkpoint/revision, context limit, reasoning/effort setting, decoding parameters, seed if supported, wrapper/executable hash, prompt-template and rendered-input hashes, tool policy, network/sandbox state, clean-room/context-lineage ID. Null model identity is an audit failure unless the route is non-model. |
| **Lifecycle** | Retry allowance and ordinal; queue/launch/start/end timestamps; setup/run/drain/cleanup durations; monotonic deadline; PID/pidfd/start-time/session/group/cgroup-or-unit identity; terminal cause; signal/exit code; heartbeats/checkpoints; retry trigger, material change, and disposition. |
| **Compute** | Input/output/cached/reasoning tokens where reported; provider usage; wall/CPU/GPU/tool/Lean time; peak RSS; values labeled measured, estimated, or unknown; monetary cost and missing-data reason; aggregate budget before/after. Timeout ceilings are never recorded as consumption. |
| **Inputs** | Content-addressed source inventory; target/contract, plan/project/outcome digests; executable/toolchain hashes; working-directory capability; environment-policy digest; before-workspace/Merkle root; source locator/version/license where applicable. |
| **Outputs** | Bounded stdout/stderr plus full-stream hashes; byte count and truncation status; output schema; artifact hashes/sizes/canonical run-relative locators; after-workspace root; added/modified/deleted manifest; freshness token; publication result; retention/checkpoint state. |
| **Validation** | Parser/validator version; exact machine codes and JSON paths; replay commands with argv/cwd/environment digest; exit status and stream hashes; Lean declaration/type hash, build/axiom receipt; certificate replay; mutation outcomes; verifier identity; replay recipe. |
| **Mathematical evidence** | Claim/content hash separate from campaign instance; target and obligation scopes; evidence class; assumptions/domain/quantifiers; dependencies; producer/verifier edges; one verdict per claim; disagreements/adjudication; semantic relation/issues; limitations; deterministic closure result. |
| **Separated status** | `controller_status`, `execution_status`, `artifact_status`, `verification_status`, `semantic_status`, `scientific_status`, `gate_decision`, `outcome_reason`; timestamps for execution end, validation, classification, strategy selection, and administrative closure. |
| **Scheduler** | Ready set; selected task; capacity/`max_parallel`; resource conflict; queue duration; starvation clock; preemption/cancellation reason; dependency state; reason each ready independent root was or was not launched. |
| **Completeness** | Expected record/stage manifest; present/missing/malformed counts; first/last sequence; whole-run or partial-slice declaration; telemetry completeness. `complete=true` is prohibited unless every required hash and terminal record verifies. |

A capability or model catalogue may contain an abstract **cost weight** for routing and budgeting. That catalogue value is **not dollars and must not be reported as monetary cost**. Monetary cost is populated only from an attributable provider billing/usage receipt or left `unknown` with a reason; timeouts, token ceilings, catalogue weights, and estimated resource classes are not converted into dollars without an explicit, versioned pricing source.

### 10.7 Watchdogs, scheduling, and benchmark acceptance for routing

- **No-output watchdog:** retain heartbeat/tool activity and checkpoint by the earlier of 10 minutes or 25% of the task deadline; terminate or checkpoint a no-progress invocation by the earlier of 20 minutes or 50% of the deadline. Retry requires a passing health probe or material configuration change.
- **Scheduler fairness:** every dispatch records the ready set and selection. If a required independent root waits longer than the lesser of 5 minutes or 20% of its phase budget while capacity exists, emit `STARVATION_POLICY_VIOLATION` and schedule it or record a typed resource conflict.
- **Per-obligation limits:** Luna ≤2 calls; Terra ≤2 producer methods plus ≤1 independent verification; Sol Planner ≤1 initial plus ≤1 field-level revision; Sol invention ≤2 attempts; Sol adjudication ≤1, with a second independent Sol only for `critical` impact. Human review count is explicit. Overrides require a human-signed budget amendment.

Routing-policy acceptance uses a frozen, content-addressed incident corpus and must satisfy:

1. **Router conformance:** 100% of safety-critical schema/config/path/DAG/journal/metrics/CLI/exact-tool cases remain deterministic; bounded rendering/extraction routes Luna; ordinary code, execution-kernel, and routine Lean reasoning route Terra; only validated frontier planning/invention or critical mathematical adjudication routes Sol; semantic/security/publication authority routes human.
2. **Sol legality:** 100% of Sol calls carry a passing frontier or adjudication receipt; known calibration cases—Bézout, cubic divisibility, dependent system, geometric series, missing evidence, odd-prime counterexample, quadratic minimum, redacted mean, and signed cross multiplication—use deterministic-first and at most Terra for residual prose/routine proof repair. Any Sol call on those cases is a routing failure.
3. **Planner behavior:** no execution while `adopted_plan_digest` is null; fail-open gates are rejected; local defects use field-level repair; plan lifecycle events are complete; accepted plans require at most one revision; zero target substitutions and 100% mandatory-obligation coverage.
4. **Timeout/retry behavior:** staged replays of historical 643–3,602 second incidents produce watchdog/checkpoint and terminal receipts, reaped process trees, useful retained partials where valid, one receipt per invocation, exact retry ordinals, and no retry without a health/configuration delta.
5. **Scheduler behavior:** ready sets, capacity use, queue bounds, starvation enforcement, and unaffected-cone continuation are deterministically exercised with barriers, not sleeps.
6. **False-closure veto:** a model `solved` claim paired with failed external verification yields `verification_status=failed` and never solved. Process success, compiled proof, semantic review, and mathematical status remain separately scored.
7. **Diversity attacks:** same-model, shared-context, same-checkpoint, missing-identity, producer-as-reviewer, and producer-as-adjudicator scenarios produce `independence_status=failed`; approved clean-room diversity paths produce `satisfied` or a specifically approved degraded result.
8. **Frontier calibration:** use at least 20 frozen obligations spanning known-playbook hard problems, genuine unresolved frontier mathematics, and high-impact conflicting evidence. Report classification outcomes and Sol precision/recall, while enforcing hard invariants independently: no ineligible Sol call and no automatic closure from Sol prose. Initial overall classification target is at least 90%, with threshold changes retained rather than hidden through fallback.
9. **Security and durability:** all applicable P0 publication, journal, process identity, sandbox, stale handoff, redaction, verified-import, evidence, and benchmark-isolation contracts pass under the prescribed layer/capability matrix.
10. **Accounting:** per obligation and tier report calls, successful/failed/superseded duration, retries, queue time, tokens, attributable monetary cost or `unknown`, retained novel artifact bytes, verifier yield, closure yield, and telemetry completeness. Release requires zero unreceipted invocations, zero unexplained model identities, zero execution from null plans, zero fail-open continuations, and zero false closures.

## 11. Playbooks: executable mathematical SOPs

### Definition

**PlaybookV2** is an executable, immutable, semantically versioned, evidence-gated mathematical standard operating procedure. It is a registered program over typed artifacts: the controller resolves inputs by digest, evaluates preconditions, compiles a deterministic step DAG, assigns role-constrained executors, records every invocation, validates every output against a named schema, computes audit independence, applies invalidation transitively, and derives a terminal result. A playbook is not prompt text: prompts, when needed, are versioned step resources whose hashes are inputs to an otherwise controller-owned protocol. Playbooks never determine campaign success directly; they emit evidence and typed decisions consumed by deterministic obligation closure. V1 readers remain quarantined, read-only migration inputs, and no playbook may emit a v1 record.

### Versioned schema

- **Schema identity.** All native records use `{artifact_type, schema_version, producer_version}`. `schema_version` is SemVer. A registry key is `(artifact_type, major_version)`; exact minor/patch readers and migrations are declared. Unknown major versions fail closed. Minor additions are accepted only in a declared `extensions` object; core objects otherwise reject unknown and missing fields. Canonical encoding is strict I-JSON UTF-8 with sorted keys, finite numbers only, non-boolean integers, no duplicate keys, and deterministic newline policy.
- **`PlaybookDefinitionV2`** (`artifact_type=playbook_definition`, `schema_version=2.0.0`) required fields: `playbook_id: Identifier`, `revision: SemVer`, `title`, `purpose`, `risk_class:{routine,high,critical}`, `capability_ids:[Identifier]`, `input_schema_refs:[SchemaRef]`, `parameter_schema_ref`, `preconditions:[PredicateSpec]`, `steps:[StepSpec]`, `outputs:[OutputSpec]`, `gates:[GateSpec]`, `invalidation:InvalidationSpec`, `audit:DiversitySpec`, `budget:BudgetSpec`, `failure_policy:FailurePolicySpec`, `receipt_profile:ReceiptProfileRef`, `compatibility`, `implementation_digest:Sha256`, `status:{draft,approved,deprecated,withdrawn}`, `approved_by`, and `approval_receipt`. Step and gate IDs are unique; the step DAG is known and acyclic; every mandatory output has at least one producing step and validating gate; every terminal path reaches exactly one result derivation.
- **`SchemaRefV2`.** `{artifact_type, major_version, exact_version?, registry_digest}`. Resolution is immediate during run compilation. A definition cannot be adopted with unresolved schema, feature, handoff, tool, or verifier registrations.
- **`PredicateSpecV2`.** `{predicate_id, evaluator_id, evaluator_version, inputs:[BindingRef], expected:{true|false|enum|range}, failure_code, evidence_required:[EvidenceClass], cache_key_fields}`. Predicates are pure, bounded, and controller-evaluated. Model prose cannot satisfy a precondition.
- **`StepSpecV2`.** `{step_id, kind:StepKind, depends_on:[step_id], mandatory:boolean, executor_role, capability_ids, input_bindings:[BindingRef], output_bindings:[OutputBinding], command_or_protocol_ref, timeout_policy_id, retry_policy_id, checkpoint_policy_id, mutation_policy:{none,scratch_only,artifact_transaction,resource_reconcile}, evidence_class, falsifies:[claim_or_obligation_id], on_success, on_failure}`. Tools, model tier, timeout, retries, parallelism, and failure transitions are compiled by the controller, never accepted from model output.
- **`OutputSpecV2`.** `{output_id, schema_ref, producer_step_id, cardinality:{one,optional_one,many_nonempty}, canonical_hash_domain, required_bindings:[campaign_id,run_id,playbook_id,playbook_revision,run_nonce,input_root,contract_digest,producer_step_id,attempt_id], retention:{mandatory,checkpoint,diagnostic}, evidence_class, validators:[ValidatorRef], consumers:[Identifier]}`. No pathname or arbitrary evidence string can substitute for an `ArtifactRefV2`.
- **`GateSpecV2`.** `{gate_id, after_steps:[step_id], gate_kind:{preflight,artifact_validation,formal_verification,independent_audit,semantic_approval,continuation,closure,publication_recovery}, mandatory, predicates:[PredicateSpec], required_outputs:[output_id], required_receipts:[ReceiptSelector], independence_policy_id?, decision_schema_ref, pass_transition, fail_transition}`. Mandatory gates are fail-closed. Continuation requires an explicit digest-bound `continue`; absence, malformed output, timeout, `continue` failure policy, or process exit 0 is not continuation.
- **`InvalidationSpecV2`.** `{dependency_fields:[BindingRef], invalidation_triggers:[TriggerSpec], scope_algorithm:{direct,transitive_dag,custom_registered}, preserve:[ArtifactClass], tombstone_schema_ref, approval_invalidation:boolean, review_invalidation:boolean, replay_required:[ValidatorRef]}`. Mandatory trigger set includes any change to target/contract, source or input root, playbook revision or implementation digest, schema registry digest, effective policy, toolchain/executable, allowed axioms, producer output digest, verifier rubric, semantic declaration binding, or approval subject. Invalidated artifacts are retained and marked `STALE`; they are never silently rewritten or deleted.
- **`DiversitySpecV2`.** `{producer_requirements, verifier_requirements, adjudicator_requirements, identity_dimensions:[provider,model_family,checkpoint,organization,principal_id,context_lineage], minimum_distinct_dimensions, clean_room_required, writable_source_forbidden, exception_policy, computation_version}`. `independence_status` is controller-computed as `satisfied`, `degraded_with_approved_exception`, or `failed`; self-asserted independence is ignored.
- **`BudgetSpecV2`.** Hard limits for `steps`, `attempts_per_step`, `revisions`, `wall_time`, `no_progress_time`, `tokens`, `cost`, `model_tier_calls`, `parallelism`, `disk_bytes`, `output_bytes`, `verifier_invocations`, and `autonomous_rounds`. Each field has `{limit,unit,scope}`. Missing mandatory limits fail compilation. Authorization is checked before every attempt and updated from receipts; timeout ceilings are not counted as consumption.
- **`PlaybookRunV2`** (`artifact_type=playbook_run`, `schema_version=2.0.0`): `{playbook_id,playbook_revision,definition_digest,run_id,campaign_id,run_nonce,contract_digest,input_root,policy_digest,registry_digest,toolchain_digest?,compiled_step_dag,compiled_budget,frontier_eligibility_receipt?,state:{created,preflight,executing,auditing,awaiting_approval,recovering,succeeded,failed,blocked,invalidated},sequence,event_head,started_at,ended_at?,result_ref?}`. State changes occur only through typed hash-chained events with CAS sequence checks.
- **`StepReceiptV2`** (`artifact_type=step_receipt`, `schema_version=2.0.0`) has launch and terminal forms. Required common fields: campaign/run/playbook/step/attempt/invocation IDs; anti-replay nonce; prior/current event hash; route and matched playbook; executor principal and exact model/tool/executable identity; clean-room lineage; input/contract/policy/toolchain hashes; capabilities; argv/protocol and environment-policy hash; immutable workspace roots; budget before; launch intent and process/unit identity. Terminal adds typed cause, native exit/signal, monotonic timing, heartbeat/checkpoint sequence, stdout/stderr full hashes and bounded representations, truncation, output artifact refs, mutation manifest, cleanup/reaping result, measured/estimated/unknown compute, budget after, validator results, retry disposition, and signature/attestation. A launch receipt without a terminal receipt is an incident requiring reconciliation.
- **`GateDecisionV2`** (`artifact_type=gate_decision`, `schema_version=2.0.0`): `{gate_id,subject_digests,predicate_results,required_receipt_digests,independence_status,decision:{pass,fail,blocked,continue,stop},reason_codes,limitations,decider:{controller|formal_verifier|human},policy_digest,sequence,signature_or_attestation}`. Models may supply evidence but cannot be the `decider` for transition legality.
- **`ArtifactRefV2`.** `{namespace,digest,size,kind,media_type,schema_ref,producer:{run_id,playbook_id,step_id,attempt_id,invocation_id},locator,assurance_class,input_root,contract_digest,execution_attestation_digest}`. Locator is display/retrieval metadata; digest and capability-bound resolution are authoritative.
- **`PlaybookResultV2`** (`artifact_type=playbook_result`, `schema_version=2.0.0`): `{run_ref,terminal_status:{succeeded,failed,blocked,invalidated},success_code?,failure:{class,code,step_id?,gate_id?,retryable,details_ref?}?,outputs:[ArtifactRefV2],evidence_manifest_ref,receipt_manifest_ref,audit_summary,independence_status,limitations,unresolved_obligations,invalidation_head,closure_input_digest}`. `succeeded` requires all mandatory steps, outputs, receipts, validators, gates, and diversity rules; it does not mean the campaign or mathematics is solved.
- **`FailurePolicySpecV2`.** Failure classes are exactly `CONTRACT`, `SCHEMA`, `INFRASTRUCTURE`, `RESOURCE`, `EVIDENCE_INTEGRITY`, `FORMAL_REJECTION`, `MATHEMATICAL_REFUTATION`, `UNRESOLVED_MATH`, `SEMANTIC_UNCLEAR`, `POLICY_DENIAL`, `APPROVAL_REQUIRED`, `BUDGET_EXHAUSTED`, and `RECOVERY_FAILED`. Retryability and escalation are registered per code. Infrastructure, malformed output, tampering, formal rejection, and deterministic refutation never escalate to a stronger model.
- **Migration/version rule.** V1 artifacts may enter only through `v1_import` readers that preserve exact historical behavior and produce a signed `MigrationReportV2` enumerating source version, byte digest, normalized values, defaults, unsupported assumptions, and resulting v2 refs. A playbook definition revision is mandatory for semantic, gate, output-schema, invalidation, diversity, or executor-policy change; runs pin the exact definition digest.

### Controller/model split

#### Controller-owned protocol

- The controller, not a model, resolves the playbook, validates its definition, compiles the step DAG, assigns tools/models, checks budgets, schedules steps, evaluates gates, applies invalidation, and derives terminal status.
- Every playbook run is bound to immutable `contract_digest`, `input_root`, `policy_digest`, `registry_digest`, definition digest, and anti-replay nonce before preflight. A null or mutable binding blocks execution.
- Every step must have a durable launch intent/receipt before side effects and a terminal receipt in `finally`. Missing terminal receipts force `recovering`; they can never be inferred from files, process exit, or prose.
- Every declared output is schema-validated, hash-bound, attempt-scoped, atomically published, indexed, and connected to exact inputs and receipts. Existing or stale output cannot satisfy a new attempt.
- Universal gates injected into every playbook are: `U0_definition_and_binding`, `U1_preflight_and_budget`, `U2_receipt_completeness`, `U3_output_schema_and_hash`, `U4_invalidation_freshness`, `U5_independent_audit`, `U6_terminal_derivation`. A playbook cannot remove or weaken them.
- `U0` checks approved definition status, versions, implementation digest, exact input and contract bindings, known registrations, DAG acyclicity, and output/gate reachability.
- `U1` runs deterministic falsification, capability/toolchain preflight, source/evidence replay, path confinement, budget authorization, and checks that no side effect occurred before pass.
- `U2` requires one launch and one terminal receipt for every invocation, reconciled process/resource identity, complete expected-record manifest, and explicit unknown telemetry. Zero-output or interrupted attempts remain visible.
- `U3` validates exact output schema, cardinality, binding fields, digest/size, atomic publication, mutation policy, and retained evidence completeness. Output parser success on bytes different from hashed bytes is forbidden.
- `U4` recomputes the dependency fingerprint and transitive invalidation closure immediately before audit and terminal derivation. Changed dependencies tombstone outputs, revoke approvals, and reset affected reviews.
- `U5` computes independence from identities and context lineage, enforces claim-complete verdict coverage, and requires formal/exact authority where available. Failed or degraded-unapproved diversity blocks success.
- `U6` derives result from mandatory step/gate/output states. Process success, model confidence, generic `PASS`, configured completion, or publication cannot set playbook success.
- Required mathematical routine audit is a producer principal plus separately launched clean-room Terra verifier plus deterministic/formal checks where available. High-impact review must differ in at least two registered identity dimensions where available; approved exceptions are digest-bound and cannot omit formal checks.
- Sol is never an executor, verifier of its own plan, scheduler, gate decider, or closure authority. Sol may only emit a typed plan or one evidence-linked revision after a controller-issued frontier/adjudication eligibility receipt.
- The controller rejects target, quantifier, domain, assumption, perimeter-semantics, candidate-universe, declaration, or endpoint changes as output repair. Such changes create a new contract/content hash and require new review.
- Machine authority dominates prose: deterministic counterexample, exact checker, Lean kernel/build/axiom receipt, strict schema validator, and reproducible replay can reject any Luna/Terra/Sol result.
- Retries are obligation-local. Admission requires a typed limitation code, materially different method signature or material infrastructure correction, expected new machine-checkable artifact, and remaining budget. Whitespace, paraphrase, larger token allowance, or wrapper changes are not novelty.
- No-output watchdog is mandatory: checkpoint/health event by the earlier of 10 minutes or 25% of deadline; stop/checkpoint by the earlier of 20 minutes or 50% without progress. Retry requires a health probe or material configuration change.
- Scheduler receipts contain ready set, selected step, capacity, queue time, and resource conflict. A required independent root waiting beyond the lesser of five minutes or 20% of phase budget while capacity exists triggers a starvation violation.
- Success and failure remain orthogonal to campaign and mathematical status. A successful playbook means its SOP completed and evidence is valid; deterministic campaign closure separately decides whether obligations are solved.
- Repairs are append-only. Corrupt, stale, superseded, rejected, and failed artifacts remain retained with lineage and cannot be overwritten, averaged, or silently omitted.
- Human authority is required for authoritative informal-to-formal equivalence, contract amendments, security exceptions, destructive publication, and exceptional corruption/authenticity disputes. Human approval cannot waive kernel failure, deterministic refutation, missing evidence, or unauthorized capability.
- Canonical content excludes presentation timestamps where reproducibility requires it; publication metadata may carry time outside the content hash. All digests state their domain.
- Every run exports an expected-record manifest, receipt manifest, evidence manifest, first/last event sequence, event head, and whole-run versus partial-slice declaration. `complete=true` is forbidden unless every expected terminal record verifies.

#### Registered step kinds

- `controller.bind`: resolve definition, schemas, immutable inputs, contract, policy, registries, nonce, and capability handles.
- `controller.preflight`: deterministic schema/path/DAG/source/toolchain/budget/falsification checks with no external mutation.
- `controller.route`: choose deterministic/Luna/Terra/Sol-plan/human authority according to registered predicates and emit a routing receipt.
- `luna.execute_known`: execute a bounded deterministic or fully specified known step; Luna cannot choose truth, target, tools, path, budget, status, or continuation.
- `luna.render_extract`: bounded schema-constrained extraction or rendering from frozen evidence, followed by deterministic validation.
- `terra.maintain`: implement or repair an approved known method, proof, schema adapter, checker, or execution kernel without changing policy or target.
- `terra.reason`: ordinary mathematical/code reasoning under a known playbook, producing typed claims, proof source, counterexamples, or repair candidates.
- `terra.audit`: clean-room, nonmutating, claim-complete independent review of frozen producer artifacts.
- `sol.plan_frontier`: one typed decomposition/invention plan; legal only with `frontier_eligible=true`.
- `sol.revise_frontier_plan`: one field-level, validator-directed revision of a prior Sol plan; never a fresh unrestricted regeneration.
- `formal.verify`: Lean kernel/build/signature/axiom audit, exact arithmetic, proof-object checker, certificate replay, or deterministic counterexample authority.
- `controller.checkpoint`: atomically retain changed artifacts, progress measures, unresolved regions, logs, and resumability metadata.
- `controller.gate`: evaluate typed predicates and emit a signed/hash-bound gate decision.
- `controller.invalidate`: compute dependency closure, tombstone stale artifacts, revoke approvals, and schedule only affected descendants.
- `controller.reconcile`: recover process/unit/workspace/journal/publication intents idempotently under the run lock.
- `human.approve`: digest-bound authority action for semantics, security, publication, contract, or corruption; never routine transition execution.
- `controller.publish_artifact`: atomic retained-artifact publication after validation; destructive external publication uses a separate approved transaction playbook.
- `controller.derive_result`: deterministic terminal result derivation and manifest finalization.

#### Model and authority policy

- **Sol: planner/reviser only.** Default allowance is zero. Admission requires a controller-generated frontier receipt naming mandatory unresolved obligations, exhausted playbooks, retained materially distinct Terra attempts, independent gap confirmation, absence of deterministic/formal settlement, and remaining Sol budget. At most one plan plus one validator-linked revision; Sol cannot execute steps, approve continuation, or mark solved.
- **Terra: maintenance and ordinary reasoning.** Terra implements known mathematical/formal methods, performs routine proof repair, maintains controllers/checkers, and supplies clean-room audits. Default per obligation is at most two materially distinct producer attempts plus one independent audit. Terra does not redesign policy, change targets, or self-approve.
- **Luna: deterministic/known execution.** Luna runs fully specified commands, schema migrations, extraction, report generation, certificate replay, and other bounded steps whose outputs have deterministic oracles. At most one initial call plus one syntax/omission repair; malformed output then becomes `MODEL_CONTRACT_FAILED`, not an intelligence escalation.
- **Formal/exact verifier:** authority lane, not a model tier. It can reject any model result. Positive model review never substitutes for an available verifier.
- **Human:** narrow authority for frozen contracts, authoritative semantic equivalence, security exceptions, destructive publication, and corruption/authenticity adjudication. Human actions are schema-validated and digest-bound.
- Escalation Luna→Terra occurs only for valid but substantively insufficient output requiring cross-artifact/code or mathematical reasoning. Blank output, malformed JSON, timeout, missing dependency, sandbox failure, or tampering routes to deterministic recovery.
- Escalation Terra→Sol occurs only for `UNRESOLVED_MATH` on a mandatory primary-target obligation after evidence-backed frontier eligibility. Difficult-looking implementation work, process recovery, schemas, sandboxing, publication, routine Lean repair, and known certificate methods are never Sol work.
- Sol adjudication, if separately registered, uses a third identity distinct from Planner/producer and only for high-impact schema-valid mathematical conflicts that deterministic replay cannot decide. Human handles semantic target-changing disputes.
- Model outputs contain content and declared capability needs only. The controller overwrites or rejects model-supplied tools, model names, timeouts, attempts, parallelism, failure policies, paths, budgets, status, and gate decisions.
- No model may upgrade evidence class. Prose remains prose; source-derived remains source-derived; interval remains interval; a Lean/exact audited label requires the corresponding controller-authenticated receipt.

### Evidence contract

- Evidence classes are distinct and nonfungible: `formal_audited`, `exact_checked`, `proof_object_checked`, `interval_certified`, `deterministically_verified`, `source_derived`, `semantic_human_approved`, `independent_model_review`, `model_proposal`, `numeric_telemetry`, and `operational_receipt`. No averaging or rhetorical promotion is allowed.
- Every evidence item is an `ArtifactRefV2` bound to campaign/run/playbook/step/attempt, immutable inputs, contract digest, producer identity, execution attestation, schema, digest, size, and assurance class.
- Formal evidence requires clean pinned build, exact declaration signature/type hash, source and dependency roots, compiler/kernel/toolchain identity, complete untruncated output or authenticated report channel, axiom/unsafe/sorry scan, replay recipe, and terminal receipt. Compilation does not establish source fidelity or semantic equivalence.
- Certificate evidence requires a generated manifest of domains, branches, exact witnesses, margins, coverage cells, joins/endpoints, checker/proof-object version, source generator digest, deterministic replay, and mutation tests. Numeric telemetry is retained separately and cannot close uncovered regions.
- Claim evidence records content hash separately from campaign claim ID; exact statement, assumptions, domains, quantifiers, dependencies, target mapping, evidence refs, producer, verifier verdicts, disagreements, adjudication, limitations, and closure state.
- Semantic evidence binds target ID, informal source digest, declaration identity/type digest, toolchain digest, campaign/run IDs, reviewer identity and independence, relation, typed issues/locations, and authoritative human approval when required.
- Receipts separate `controller_status`, `execution_status`, `artifact_status`, `verification_status`, `semantic_status`, `scientific_status`, `gate_decision`, and `outcome_reason`. One dimension never implies another.
- Evidence is complete only when the exact retained set matches the manifest under a stable locked snapshot bound to state sequence/digest and event head. Self-consistency is labeled separately from authenticity.
- Invalidation is evidence-preserving: stale evidence keeps its original status and lineage but is excluded from current closure. Fresh replay can create a new descendant; it cannot mutate the old item.
- A playbook result exposes limitations and unresolved obligations even when successful. `succeeded` means the SOP ran correctly, not that a theorem or campaign is solved.

### `math.receipt_first_formal_audit@2.0.0`

**Purpose.** Turn reported Lean/formal source claims into controller-authenticated, reproducible receipts before any `formal_audited` label. This is the first gate for CMV and any retained theorem claim.

**Preconditions**

1. Immutable repository/source root, target declaration registry, exact expected type or type-hash policy, allowed-axiom policy, pinned Lean/Lake/Mathlib/toolchain digests, and verification root are present.
2. No trusted build input or report channel is candidate-writable; capability preflight passes.
3. Claim is not already discharged by a fresh receipt with identical dependency fingerprint.

**Steps**

1. `controller.preflight`: verify source inventory, import reachability, exact declaration uniqueness, toolchain pins, report-channel integrity, output bounds, and absence of mutable trusted paths.
2. `luna.execute_known`: create/use one verification root importing every declared public target; run clean build with the pinned executable, not a project-substitutable second environment.
3. `formal.verify`: emit exact theorem signatures/type hashes; scan reachable source for `sorry`, `admit`, custom axioms, unsafe/native substitution, and forbidden constructs; run `#print axioms` or structured equivalent per declaration.
4. `luna.execute_known`: capture complete command/environment/source/dependency/output digests and replay recipe; fail on truncation or ambiguous declaration output.
5. `terra.audit`: independently compare frozen contract, declaration types, source reachability, allowed axioms, and receipt bindings; Terra cannot repair during audit.
6. `controller.derive_result`: publish audit manifest and per-declaration status without inferring source fidelity or semantic equivalence.

**Outputs**

- `FormalAuditManifestV2`: `{verification_root_ref,source_root,dependency_root,toolchain,commands:[receipt_ref],declarations:[{target_id,declaration,expected_type_hash,observed_type_hash,uniqueness,reachable,axioms,forbidden_constructs,status}],build_status,report_channel,limitations}`.
- One `LeanVerificationReceiptV3` per required declaration, plus exact source/build/output refs and a `PlaybookResultV2`. Cardinality and exact target coverage are controller-checked.

**Gates**

- Universal gates U0–U6 apply, including output schema, freshness, receipt completeness, invalidation, and independent audit.
- `F0_trust_boundary`: pinned executable/toolchain and nonwritable trusted source/report channel.
- `F1_clean_build`: actual target-including clean build succeeds with complete output.
- `F2_signature`: exact declaration exists once and type hash matches.
- `F3_axioms`: axiom set is within policy; no forbidden proof holes/unsafe path.
- `F4_audit_diversity`: Terra auditor is clean-room and distinct from any proof author; formal verifier receipt is mandatory.
- Success: every mandatory declaration passes F0–F4 and manifests verify. Failure: typed formal rejection, integrity failure, infrastructure block, or contract mismatch; no model escalation.

**Default tiers**

- Luna executes pinned build/scan/report steps.
- Terra performs ordinary independent contract-to-receipt audit.
- Sol disabled.

**Escalation**

- Infrastructure/receipt defects → deterministic recovery only.
- Routine proof break with unchanged target → Terra maintenance playbook.
- Substantive mandatory frontier gap after two valid Terra methods and independent confirmation → controller may admit Sol planning, never audit execution.
- Semantic mismatch between exact theorem and informal target → human semantic review; no automatic success.

### `math.typed_claim_dag_reconciliation@2.0.0`

**Purpose.** Reconcile reports, promotion records, corrections, formal receipts, interval results, and model claims into one authority-aware typed claim DAG without laundering scope or evidence class.

**Preconditions**

1. Frozen artifact inventory with raw-byte digests and registered parsers.
2. Authority policy and permanent-correction registry are approved.
3. Every imported v1 record has a `MigrationReportV2`; malformed records are quarantined.

**Steps**

1. `luna.execute_known`: parse and normalize claim statements while preserving exact quantifiers, domains, assumptions, perimeter semantics, candidate universe, endpoint strictness, and evidence class.
2. `controller.preflight`: derive content hashes, validate unique IDs, dependencies, supersession edges, no cycles, no duplicate target aliases, and exact provenance.
3. `terra.reason`: map genuinely ambiguous cross-report references and propose explicit `supports|contradicts|supersedes|limits|depends_on` edges; no statement rewriting.
4. `formal.verify`: replay all attached exact/formal/checker evidence and downgrade nothing silently.
5. `terra.audit`: claim-complete clean-room review of authority ordering, permanent corrections, source/model separation, and all contradictions.
6. `controller.derive_result`: deterministically compute current/noncurrent claims, unresolved conflicts, and generated status/documentation views.

**Outputs**

- `TypedClaimDagV2`: `{claims:[{content_hash,instance_id,statement,scope,assumptions,domains,quantifiers,evidence_class,authority,dependencies,evidence_refs,limitations,status}],edges,corrections,conflicts,unresolved,policy_digest}`.
- `ClaimReconciliationReportV2`: per input artifact parse status, migration, adopted/rejected/superseded claims, disagreement records, and generated documentation refs.

**Gates**

- Universal U0–U6 enforce receipts, schema, freshness, invalidation, diversity, and terminal derivation.
- `C0_closed_inventory`: every supplied artifact is parsed or quarantined with reason.
- `C1_scope_preservation`: no dropped hypothesis, domain, branch, semantics, or endpoint condition.
- `C2_authority`: audit overlays and permanent corrections are applied; prose cannot supersede stronger receipts.
- `C3_graph`: IDs/edges are unique, known, acyclic, and target coverage is explicit.
- `C4_independent_audit`: Terra verifier covers exactly all claims and conflicts.
- Success means a valid reconciled DAG may still contain unresolved claims. Failure means structural ambiguity, unbound evidence, or unresolved authority conflict prevents authoritative projection.

**Default tiers**

- Luna performs deterministic import/normalization.
- Terra handles ordinary cross-artifact mapping and clean-room audit.
- Sol disabled.

**Escalation**

- Malformed/schema/path failures → deterministic quarantine, not model escalation.
- Ambiguous ordinary reference mapping → one additional Terra method.
- High-impact mathematical conflict with valid evidence that replay cannot settle → Sol adjudication only under a separate eligibility receipt.
- Target/source interpretation change → human authority.

### `math.proof_producing_certificate_generation@2.0.0`

**Purpose.** Convert expensive exact-decimal, rational interval, or bounded numerical arguments into generated manifests and proof objects/checkable Lean certificates, replacing smoke scripts and hand-maintained ladders.

**Preconditions**

1. Canonical scalar/spec AST and exact domain partition are frozen.
2. Target inequality/equality, endpoint policy, branches, and allowed arithmetic primitives are typed.
3. A small registered proof-object checker or Lean replay interface exists; unsound `verify_full_domain.py` is explicitly forbidden.

**Steps**

1. `controller.preflight`: exact arithmetic sanity, branch/domain completeness, counterexample search, endpoint/join inventory, and tool/ABI pins.
2. `terra.reason`: maintain the approved certificate decomposition and generate exact witness requirements; no target or domain changes.
3. `luna.execute_known`: generate rational/interval cells, directed bounds, witness margins, and canonical certificate manifest from the frozen spec.
4. `formal.verify`: replay each cell and join in Lean or the registered proof-object checker; run mutation tests removing/changing every mandatory witness.
5. `controller.checkpoint`: retain completed cells, unresolved regions, generator/checker logs, and resumable manifest at bounded intervals.
6. `terra.audit`: independently verify coverage algebra, exact-decimal semantics, joins, strict/equality endpoints, and that telemetry is not promoted.
7. `controller.derive_result`: emit certified coverage and explicitly uncovered regions.

**Outputs**

- `CertificateManifestV2`: `{spec_digest,generator_digest,checker_digest,toolchain,domains,branches,cells:[{domain,witnesses,margins,proof_ref,status}],joins,endpoints,coverage_root,uncovered,mutation_results,replay_receipts}`.
- `ProofProducingCertificateV2` or Lean artifact refs, plus separate `NumericTelemetryV2`; never one mixed evidence object.

**Gates**

- Universal U0–U6 apply.
- `P0_preflight`: no deterministic counterexample and all domains/branches/endpoints are enumerated.
- `P1_proof_replay`: every claimed cell and join has a successful untruncated formal/exact receipt.
- `P2_coverage`: union of certified cells exactly equals the declared claimed domain; uncovered set must be empty for global claims.
- `P3_mutation`: removal or corruption of each mandatory witness causes rejection.
- `P4_diversity`: Terra audit is independent of generator maintenance; formal checker is authoritative.
- Success may be bounded (`global_coverage=false`) or global; output status must say which. Any uncovered region forbids a global theorem claim.

**Default tiers**

- Luna executes generation, replay, and checkpoint steps.
- Terra maintains decomposition and performs ordinary audit.
- Sol disabled by default.

**Escalation**

- Generator/checker/infrastructure defects → deterministic or Terra maintenance.
- Failure of an existing decomposition with a mathematical `UNRESOLVED_MATH` region → two materially distinct Terra methods first.
- Sol may plan a new decomposition only after frontier eligibility; Sol never supplies accepted certificate cells without formal replay.

### `math.equal_area_fold_root_topology@2.0.0`

**Purpose.** Prove complete equal-area root topology, fold behavior, least-perimeter root selection, and endpoint cases without treating a numerical continuation path as complete evidence.

**Preconditions**

1. Canonical E3/E4 scalar formulas, branch tags, domains, and exact equal-weighted-area semantics are frozen.
2. Derivative/fold primitives and source versus modeled scope are explicitly distinguished.
3. Existing exact-human reductions and interval evidence are retained by digest.

**Steps**

1. `controller.preflight`: symbolically/deterministically validate formulas, dimensions, domains, branch transitions, and known endpoint degenerations; search for counterexamples.
2. `terra.reason`: implement the approved topology proof obligations: fold locations, monotonic intervals, complete root multiplicity, descending-root existence, second-root criterion, endpoint equality, and least-perimeter selection.
3. `luna.execute_known`: run exact algebra, generated derivative-sign checks, bounded interval sublemmas, and Lean compilation/checks.
4. `formal.verify`: verify each exported topology declaration and dependency/axiom receipt.
5. `terra.audit`: independent claim-by-claim audit, including h=1/2 corrected degeneration, h=1 closure-only rule, and no single-root completeness assumption.
6. `controller.derive_result`: publish modeled or source-scoped results exactly as proved.

**Outputs**

- `EqualAreaTopologyBundleV2`: `{spec_digest,scope:{modeled|source},branches,folds,root_set_theorems,selection_theorems,endpoints,formal_receipts,counterexample_receipts,limitations}`.
- Per-obligation proof refs for existence, completeness, multiplicity, least-perimeter selection, and endpoint behavior.

**Gates**

- Universal U0–U6 apply.
- `E0_contract`: exact equal-area semantics and branch/domain bindings match.
- `E1_completeness`: every branch and endpoint obligation has proof or explicit unresolved status; sampled roots cannot satisfy it.
- `E2_formal`: required declarations pass exact type/build/axiom audit.
- `E3_scope`: modeled result cannot be labeled source result.
- `E4_audit`: independent Terra coverage and theorem-statement audit.
- Success is obligation-specific; unresolved branches remain open and prevent downstream global continuation.

**Default tiers**

- Terra handles ordinary proof implementation and audit.
- Luna executes exact/formal known steps.
- Sol disabled until evidence-backed frontier escalation.

**Escalation**

- Routine Lean/algebra repair → Terra.
- A mandatory topology obligation that survives two materially distinct Terra methods plus clean-room gap confirmation → Sol plan eligibility.
- Any change to equal-area or source semantics → human contract review.

### `math.frontier_to_reduced_boundary@2.0.0`

**Purpose.** Bridge explicit modeled topological-frontier H¹ perimeter to source finite-perimeter/BV reduced-boundary weighted perimeter, including interfaces, joins, cracks, traces, and null modifications.

**Preconditions**

1. Receipt-audited explicit carriers and topological-frontier theorems exist.
2. Source competitor class, density trace convention, weighted reduced-boundary perimeter, representative convention, and regularity hypotheses are frozen by human-approved contract.
3. Mandatory frontier eligibility is issued if no registered known theorem/playbook discharges the bridge.

**Steps**

1. `controller.preflight`: inventory carrier regularity, interface sets, overlaps, seam endpoints, density regions, representative/null-set policy, and existing geometric-measure lemmas.
2. `sol.plan_frontier`: only when admitted, produce a typed obligation decomposition and falsification pilots for finite perimeter, reduced-boundary identification, H¹-a.e. equivalence, trace weighting, and weighted-integral equality.
3. `controller.gate`: validate Sol plan schema, target fidelity, DAG, evidence classes, pilot falsifiers, and budget; at most one validator-directed revision.
4. `terra.reason`: implement the adopted proof plan obligation by obligation, using known geometric-measure/BV methods; preserve source/model distinctions.
5. `luna.execute_known`: run static contracts, exact carrier checks, Lean builds, axiom receipts, and generated overlap/nullity tests.
6. `terra.audit`: independent audit of each bridge theorem and all interface conventions.
7. `human.approve`: authoritative semantic review that the formal definitions match the frozen source perimeter semantics.
8. `controller.derive_result`: emit separate finite-perimeter, boundary-identification, and weighted-equivalence results.

**Outputs**

- `BoundaryBridgeBundleV2`: `{source_contract_digest,modeled_carrier_root,obligations:[{id,statement_hash,proof_ref,formal_receipt,status}],interface_trace_policy,null_modification_policy,semantic_review_ref,limitations}`.
- Distinct theorem refs for finite perimeter, reduced-boundary characterization, frontier equivalence H¹-a.e., and weighted-perimeter equality; no monolithic ambiguous bridge flag.
- If Sol is invoked: `FrontierPlanV2` with lifecycle receipts `generated→validated→accepted|abandoned` and exact field diff for one revision.

**Gates**

- Universal U0–U6 apply.
- `B0_frontier_admission`: Sol step is illegal without the controller eligibility receipt; otherwise use a registered deterministic/Terra plan.
- `B1_plan_adoption`: no proof step starts while adopted plan digest is null.
- `B2_formal_components`: each component theorem passes receipt-first formal audit.
- `B3_weighted_equivalence`: interface density and trace conventions are explicit and theorem-bound.
- `B4_semantic_authority`: independent human review binds formal and source definitions.
- `B5_diversity`: Terra producer/auditor are clean-room distinct; Sol, if used, is not auditor or executor.
- Success requires all mandatory bridge components and semantic approval. Partial component success is retained but cannot promote a source theorem.

**Default tiers**

- Terra maintains and implements ordinary proof obligations.
- Luna executes deterministic/formal steps.
- Sol has no default call; one plan plus one revision only after frontier admission.
- Human owns final source-semantic equivalence.

**Escalation**

- Infrastructure/formal receipt defects → deterministic recovery/Terra maintenance.
- Unresolved mandatory bridge mathematics after applicable playbooks and two Terra methods → Sol planning under existing frontier receipt.
- Conflicting high-impact proofs → separate Sol adjudication only if replay cannot settle; semantic disagreement → human.

### `math.full_domain_gamma_certificate@2.0.0`

**Purpose.** Replace the conditional human+MPFR low/compact/large synthesis with one gap-free, proof-producing Γ<0 result over all declared λ>1, with explicit joins at 17/16 and 3/2 and uniform h-boundary handling.

**Preconditions**

1. Canonical Γ specification and relation to selected equal-area root are frozen.
2. Low-density scaled argument, compact directed-MPFR certificate, and large-density argument are imported as scoped evidence, not global proof.
3. Equal-area topology dependencies required by Γ are fresh or explicitly listed as blockers.

**Steps**

1. `controller.preflight`: replay the compact certificate, validate MPFR/ABI provenance, exact endpoints, formula identity, and search all join/boundary regions for counterexamples.
2. `terra.reason`: maintain three proof-producing lanes—low density `(1,17/16]`, compact `[17/16,3/2]`, and large `[3/2,∞)`—with explicit uniform h control and exact overlap obligations.
3. `luna.execute_known`: generate/replay rational or Lean certificates for each lane and exact join statements; checkpoint completed subdomains.
4. `formal.verify`: verify every lane, endpoint, overlap, and final union theorem using the registered formal checker.
5. `terra.audit`: independently audit coverage, strictness, equal-area-locus dependence, and prohibition on `verify_full_domain.py`.
6. `controller.derive_result`: publish global result only if exact union coverage closes; otherwise publish bounded certified regions and blockers.

**Outputs**

- `GammaCoverageV2`: `{gamma_spec_digest,root_selection_digest,lanes:[{domain,method_signature,certificate_ref,formal_receipt,status}],joins,endpoints,h_boundary_policy,coverage_proof_ref,global_coverage,uncovered,limitations}`.
- Separate formal theorem ref for the all-domain union only when `global_coverage=true`; numeric plots and mpmath checks remain `NumericTelemetryV2`.

**Gates**

- Universal U0–U6 apply.
- `G0_dependencies`: equal-area selection and Γ reduction receipts are fresh.
- `G1_lane_proofs`: all three lane results are proof-producing and formally replayed.
- `G2_joins`: 17/16 and 3/2 endpoints/overlaps are exact and gap-free.
- `G3_uniformity`: h-boundary and all declared branches are covered.
- `G4_global_union`: controller exact-set proof establishes domain equality; otherwise global claim is forbidden.
- `G5_audit`: independent Terra audit and formal authority.
- Success can be partial or global but the output schema and downstream gate distinguish them; only global success authorizes an all-λ modeled descendant.

**Default tiers**

- Terra implements known lane methods and audit.
- Luna runs generators, exact checks, replay, and checkpoints.
- Sol disabled unless a lane is evidence-backed `UNRESOLVED_MATH` after Terra exhaustion.

**Escalation**

- MPFR/ABI/checker failure → infrastructure recovery, no Sol.
- A failed lane due to deterministic counterexample → mathematical refutation/contract review, no escalation.
- A mandatory unresolved lane after two materially distinct Terra methods and audit → Sol plan eligibility for that lane only.

### `ops.no_output_process_recovery@2.0.0`

**Purpose.** Recover expensive blank, hung, interrupted, leader-dead, or unreceipted invocations without stale output reuse, descendant leaks, cost erasure, or intelligence-tier escalation.

**Preconditions**

1. A launch receipt or durable launch intent exists, or the incident is explicitly `orphan_unbound`.
2. Run lock and resource ledger are available; process/unit identity service can validate PID/start time/pgrp/session/boot ID or fail closed.
3. Attempt output namespace and pre-attempt immutable root are known.

**Steps**

1. `controller.reconcile`: freeze new dispatch, read authoritative event head, correlate launch intent, heartbeat, process/unit/resource identities, and attempt namespace.
2. `luna.execute_known`: run identity-safe liveness checks; never signal on unverifiable or mismatched identity.
3. `controller.checkpoint`: if safe, atomically retain partial changed artifacts, stream hashes, progress markers, and unresolved work before termination.
4. `controller.reconcile`: terminate unit/process group within global deadline, reap, classify survivors/errors, close resource intents, and finalize the missing terminal receipt in recovery form.
5. `controller.invalidate`: remove stale attempt outputs from current namespace by tombstoning, restore/swap immutable workspace root if required, and invalidate dependent handoffs/approvals only in the affected cone.
6. `controller.gate`: classify root cause and authorize retry only with health probe or material configuration/method change and remaining budget.
7. `controller.derive_result`: return recovered, blocked-survivors, retry-authorized, retry-denied, or corruption-required status.

**Outputs**

- `InvocationRecoveryReportV2`: `{incident_id,launch_receipt_ref,identity_checks,heartbeats,partial_checkpoint_ref?,termination_attempts,survivors,cleanup,synthetic_terminal_receipt_ref,invalidation_set,retry_decision,cost_accounting,limitations}`.
- A terminal `StepReceiptV2` marked `recovered=true`, never fabricated success; stale output tombstones and resource-ledger receipts.

**Gates**

- Universal U0–U6 apply; independent audit is deterministic identity/reconciliation verification plus Terra review for critical incidents.
- `R0_identity`: every signaled member is revalidated; mismatch/unverifiable state fails closed.
- `R1_checkpoint`: partial data is hash-bound or explicitly unavailable before destructive cleanup.
- `R2_reap`: no success while owned survivors remain; exact survivor/error list required.
- `R3_stale_output`: prior output/handoff cannot satisfy retry and approvals are invalidated transitively.
- `R4_retry`: material change/health probe and budget are mandatory; no escalation on blank/malformed/infrastructure failure.
- Success means incident reconciliation completed, not the original task. Failure means survivor, identity ambiguity, corruption, or cleanup failure remains.

**Default tiers**

- Luna executes known health, identity, checkpoint, and reconciliation commands.
- Terra maintains recovery implementation and independently reviews critical ambiguous incidents.
- Sol disabled.

**Escalation**

- Identity ambiguity, EPERM, sandbox/systemd failure → deterministic blocked incident and human only for exceptional ownership/corruption policy.
- Valid partial mathematical artifact → resume original Terra playbook from checkpoint if bindings remain fresh.
- No-output never causes Luna→Terra→Sol escalation by itself.

### `ops.authoritative_state_and_stale_artifact_recovery@2.0.0`

**Purpose.** Repair journal-ahead projections, torn tails, interrupted retries, and stale research/handoff/promotion evidence using the authoritative event chain and transitive dependency invalidation.

**Preconditions**

1. Run lock is held and v2 state store or read-only v1 migration snapshot is available.
2. Trusted event head/anchor and schema registry are available.
3. Only an unterminated tail is eligible for automatic truncation; malformed complete records are fatal.

**Steps**

1. `controller.reconcile`: verify event sequence/hash/predecessor chain from trusted checkpoint/head; classify tail, middle corruption, rollback, duplicate, or projection drift.
2. `luna.execute_known`: truncate only a proven unterminated tail according to policy; otherwise quarantine and stop.
3. `controller.reconcile`: reduce valid events into `RunStateV2`, compare projection source sequence/digest, and atomically republish projection from journal-ahead state.
4. `controller.invalidate`: recompute dependency closure for interrupted retry/source-checkpoint rerun; tombstone stale research products, handoffs, approvals, promotions, claim/semantic evidence, and descendants consistently.
5. `luna.execute_known`: verify retained evidence exact set against the repaired state snapshot and permitted controller paths.
6. `terra.audit`: independently review critical recovery reports, invalidation closure, and absence of optimistic reconstruction.
7. `controller.derive_result`: emit repaired, no-op, quarantined-corruption, or recovery-failed.

**Outputs**

- `StateRecoveryReportV2`: `{trusted_head,verified_range,tail_action,corruption_class,reduced_state_digest,old_projection,new_projection_ref,invalidation_root,tombstones,evidence_verification,audit_ref,terminal_status}`.
- Fresh `StateProjectionV2`, invalidation/tombstone events, and evidence commit only after reconciliation.

**Gates**

- Universal U0–U6 apply.
- `S0_chain`: sequence/hash/predecessor and trusted head validate; complete-record corruption is nonrepairable automatically.
- `S1_projection`: projection is derived solely from valid events and carries exact source sequence/digest.
- `S2_invalidation`: source rerun/retry clears the complete affected dependency cone, including approvals and promoted evidence.
- `S3_evidence`: exact-set evidence verifies after repair under stable snapshot.
- `S4_audit`: critical repair has independent Terra audit; corruption exceptions require human authority.
- Success never guesses missing state. Quarantine is a valid fail-closed result, not success.

**Default tiers**

- Luna executes deterministic chain/projection/evidence operations.
- Terra maintains reducer/invalidation logic and audits critical recovery.
- Sol disabled.

**Escalation**

- Malformed complete record, head rollback, authenticity dispute, or ambiguous v1 ownership → quarantine and human corruption adjudication.
- Schema/infrastructure issue → Terra maintenance, not Sol.
- No mathematical escalation is permitted from state recovery.

### `ops.publication_transaction_recovery@2.0.0`

**Purpose.** Recover or complete a digest-approved publication transaction across prepared, swapped, committed, and rollback crash cuts without mutating unrelated evidence or regressing a durable commit.

**Preconditions**

1. Canonical run and destination locks can be acquired in declared order.
2. Complete pending/terminal receipts, plan digest, approval, source refs, destination identity/snapshot semantics, and transaction nonce are present or the transaction is quarantined.
3. Run is evidence-valid for the publication generation; human approval is bound to the exact plan and destination semantics.

**Steps**

1. `controller.preflight`: validate the entire pending receipt and all paths/identities before mutation; reject traversal, alias-lock mismatch, overlaps, symlinks, special entries, forbidden hardlinks, and stale approval.
2. `controller.reconcile`: acquire run then canonical destination lock; classify filesystem identities and receipt phase through the registered transaction reducer.
3. `luna.execute_known`: rehash source/staging/preserved entries, revalidate observed destination semantics immediately before any swap, and fsync required data/directories.
4. `controller.reconcile`: execute exactly one legal forward step—prepare, swap, verify, commit-durable, clean—or rollback only before irreversible commit.
5. `human.approve`: required only for a new/recomputed plan, semantic destination change, or exceptional recovery choice; existing valid approval is reused by digest.
6. `terra.audit`: independent review for critical recovery or any committed-recovery-required outcome.
7. `controller.derive_result`: emit `committed`, `committed_recovery_required`, `rolled_back`, `failed`, or `quarantined`; refresh evidence only for transaction-owned records after reconciliation.

**Outputs**

- `PublicationRecoveryReportV2`: `{transaction_id,plan_ref,approval_ref,initial_phase,observed_identities,locks,revalidation,actions,fsync_receipts,final_phase,outcome,unrelated_evidence_unchanged,audit_ref?}`.
- Terminal `PublicationTransactionV2` event/receipt chain with exact source/staging/destination hashes and explicit recovery outcome.

**Gates**

- Universal U0–U6 apply.
- `P0_full_receipt_preflight`: malformed or incomplete receipt causes zero mutation.
